</li>
<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Also <a href="/wiki/.eu" title=".eu">.eu</a>, shared with other <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> member states.</span>

<p><b>Slovenia</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span><span &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>-/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">sloh-<span style="font-size:90%">VEE</span>-nee-ə</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a>: <i lang="sl">Slovenija</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="sl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Slovene" title="Help:IPA/Slovene">[slɔˈʋèːnija]</a></span>),<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Republic of Slovenia</b> (Slovene: <span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-2" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-labelElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/4\/4d\/Sl-Republika_Slovenija.oga\/Sl-Republika_Slovenija.oga.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:{&quot;html&quot;:&quot;&lt;i&gt;Republika Slovenija&lt;\/i&gt;&quot;},&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Sl-Republika Slovenija.oga&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/4/4d/Sl-Republika_Slovenija.oga/Sl-Republika_Slovenija.oga.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"><i>Republika Slovenija</i></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Sl-Republika_Slovenija.oga" title="File:Sl-Republika Slovenija.oga">i</a></sup>,<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Abbreviation" title="Abbreviation">abbr.</a>: <i>RS</i><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>), is a country in southern <a href="/wiki/Central_Europe" title="Central Europe">Central Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cultures_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cultures-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> to the west, <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a> to the north, <a href="/wiki/Hungary" title="Hungary">Hungary</a> to the northeast, <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a> to the southeast, and the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a> to the southwest.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_Britannica_2021_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica_2021-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia is mostly mountainous and forested,<sup id="cite_ref-perko2008_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-perko2008-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> covers 20,271 square kilometres (7,827&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> and has a population of 2.1&#160;million (2,110,547 people).<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Slovenes" title="Slovenes">Slovenes</a> constitute over 80% of the country's population.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a>, a <a href="/wiki/South_Slavic_languages" title="South Slavic languages">South Slavic language</a>, is the official language.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_Britannica_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia has a predominantly temperate <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">continental climate</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Fallon2007_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fallon2007-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> with the exception of the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Julian_Alps" title="Julian Alps">Julian Alps</a>. A sub-mediterranean climate reaches to the northern extensions of the <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Alps" title="Dinaric Alps">Dinaric Alps</a> that traverse the country in a northwest–southeast direction. The Julian Alps in the northwest have an alpine climate.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Toward the northeastern <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Basin" title="Pannonian Basin">Pannonian Basin</a>, a continental climate is more pronounced. <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a>, the capital and <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Slovenia" title="List of cities and towns in Slovenia">largest city</a> of Slovenia, is geographically situated near the centre of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-Mestna_občina_Ljubljana_2017_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mestna_občina_Ljubljana_2017-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia has historically been the crossroads of <a href="/wiki/Slavic_languages" title="Slavic languages">Slavic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Germanic_languages" title="Germanic languages">Germanic</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Romance_languages" title="Romance languages">Romance</a> languages and cultures.<sup id="cite_ref-cultures_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cultures-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Its territory has been part of many different states: the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman Empire</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantine Empire</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Carolingian_Empire" title="Carolingian Empire">Carolingian Empire</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Republic of Venice</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Illyrian_Provinces" title="Illyrian Provinces">Illyrian Provinces</a> of Napoleon's <a href="/wiki/First_French_Empire" title="First French Empire">First French Empire</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian Empire</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austro-Hungarian</a> Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_Britannica_2021_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica_2021-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In October 1918, the Slovenes co-founded the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" title="State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">State of Slovenes, Croats, and Serbs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Trgovčević_2016_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Trgovčević_2016-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> In December 1918, they merged with the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia" title="Kingdom of Serbia">Kingdom of Serbia</a> into the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia" title="Kingdom of Yugoslavia">Kingdom of Yugoslavia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-I_feel_Slovenia_2020_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-I_feel_Slovenia_2020-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Germany</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1920%E2%80%931946)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1920–1946)">Hungary</a> occupied and annexed Slovenia, with a tiny area transferred to the <a href="/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia" title="Independent State of Croatia">Independent State of Croatia</a>, a newly declared <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi</a> <a href="/wiki/Puppet_state" title="Puppet state">puppet state</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-sečen2005_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sečen2005-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> In 1945, it again became part of <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" title="Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a>. Post-war, Yugoslavia was allied with the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc">Eastern Bloc</a>, but after the <a href="/wiki/Tito%E2%80%93Stalin_split" title="Tito–Stalin split">Tito–Stalin split</a> of 1948, it never subscribed to the <a href="/wiki/Warsaw_Pact" title="Warsaw Pact">Warsaw Pact</a>, and in 1961 it became one of the founders of the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> In June 1991, Slovenia <a href="/wiki/Independence_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Independence of Slovenia">declared independence</a> from Yugoslavia and became an independent <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Škrk1999_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Škrk1999-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia is a <a href="/wiki/Developed_country" title="Developed country">developed country</a>, with a <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy" title="World Bank high-income economy">high-income economy</a> ranking highly in the <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Heritage_Foundation_2021_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Heritage_Foundation_2021-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Gini_coefficient" title="Gini coefficient">Gini coefficient</a> rates its <a href="/wiki/Economic_inequality" title="Economic inequality">income inequality</a> among the lowest in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> It is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">OSCE</a>, the <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>, and <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Portal_GOV.SI_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Portal_GOV.SI-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The name <i>Slovenia</i> etymologically means 'land of the <a href="/wiki/Slavs" title="Slavs">Slavs</a>'. The origin of the name <i lang="en"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Slav#English" class="extiw" title="wikt:Slav">Slav</a></i> itself remains uncertain. The suffix <i>-en</i> forms a <a href="/wiki/Demonym" title="Demonym">demonym</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Slovenia" title="History of Slovenia">History of Slovenia</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prehistory_to_Slavic_settlement">Prehistory to Slavic settlement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Prehistory to Slavic settlement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Prehistory">Prehistory</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Prehistory">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti dating to <a href="/wiki/Neolithic" title="Neolithic">Neolithic</a> period is the oldest wooden wheel yet discovered.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Present-day Slovenia has been inhabited since <a href="/wiki/Prehistoric" class="mw-redirect" title="Prehistoric">prehistoric</a> times. There is evidence of <a href="/wiki/Human_settlement" title="Human settlement">human habitation</a> from around 250,000 years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Divje_Babe_flute" title="Divje Babe flute">pierced cave bear bone</a>, dating from 43100 ± 700 <a href="/wiki/Before_present" class="mw-redirect" title="Before present">BP</a>, found in 1995 in Divje Babe cave near <a href="/wiki/Cerkno" title="Cerkno">Cerkno</a>, is considered a kind of flute, and possibly the oldest musical instrument discovered in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In the 1920s and 1930s, artifacts belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Cro-Magnon" class="mw-redirect" title="Cro-Magnon">Cro-Magnon</a>, such as pierced bones, bone points, and a needle were found by archaeologist <a href="/wiki/Sre%C4%8Dko_Brodar" title="Srečko Brodar">Srečko Brodar</a> in <a href="/wiki/Potok_Cave" title="Potok Cave">Potok Cave</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated2_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2002, <a href="/wiki/Prehistoric_pile_dwellings_around_the_Alps" title="Prehistoric pile dwellings around the Alps">remains of pile dwellings</a> over 4,500 years old were discovered in the <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Marsh" title="Ljubljana Marsh">Ljubljana Marsh</a>, now protected as a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a>, along with the <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Marshes_Wooden_Wheel" class="mw-redirect" title="Ljubljana Marshes Wooden Wheel">Ljubljana Marshes Wooden Wheel</a>, the oldest wooden wheel in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Oldest_Wooden_Wheel_in_the_World_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Oldest_Wooden_Wheel_in_the_World-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> It shows that wooden wheels appeared almost simultaneously in Mesopotamia and Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-Slovenia_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Slovenia-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In the transition period between the <a href="/wiki/Bronze_Age" title="Bronze Age">Bronze Age</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Iron_Age" title="Iron Age">Iron Age</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Urnfield" class="mw-redirect" title="Urnfield">Urnfield</a> culture flourished. Archaeological remains dating from the <a href="/wiki/Hallstatt_period" class="mw-redirect" title="Hallstatt period">Hallstatt period</a> have been found, particularly in southeastern Slovenia, among them a number of <a href="/wiki/Situla_(vessel)" class="mw-redirect" title="Situla (vessel)">situlas</a> in <a href="/wiki/Novo_Mesto" title="Novo Mesto">Novo Mesto</a>, the "Town of Situlas".<sup id="cite_ref-google2008_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-google2008-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Roman_era">Roman era</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Roman era">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The area that is present-day Slovenia was in Roman times shared between <i>Venetia et Histria</i> (region X of <a href="/wiki/Italia_(Roman_Empire)" class="mw-redirect" title="Italia (Roman Empire)">Roman Italia</a> in the classification of <a href="/wiki/Augustus" title="Augustus">Augustus</a>) and the provinces <a href="/wiki/Pannonia" title="Pannonia">Pannonia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Noricum" title="Noricum">Noricum</a>. The Romans established posts at <a href="/wiki/Emona" title="Emona">Emona</a> (Ljubljana), <a href="/wiki/Poetovio" class="mw-redirect" title="Poetovio">Poetovio</a> (Ptuj), and <a href="/wiki/Celeia" class="mw-redirect" title="Celeia">Celeia</a> (Celje); and constructed trade and military roads that ran across Slovene territory from Italy to Pannonia. In the 5th and 6th centuries, the area was subject to invasions by the <a href="/wiki/Huns" title="Huns">Huns</a> and Germanic tribes during their incursions into Italy. A part of the inner state was protected with a defensive line of towers and walls called <i><a href="/wiki/Claustra_Alpium_Iuliarum" title="Claustra Alpium Iuliarum">Claustra Alpium Iuliarum</a></i>. A <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Frigidus" title="Battle of the Frigidus">crucial battle</a> between <a href="/wiki/Theodosius_I" title="Theodosius I">Theodosius I</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eugenius" title="Eugenius">Eugenius</a> took place in the <a href="/wiki/Vipava_Valley" title="Vipava Valley">Vipava Valley</a> in 394.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Slavic_settlement">Slavic settlement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Slavic settlement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Slavic_peoples" class="mw-redirect" title="Slavic peoples">Slavic</a> tribes migrated to the Alpine area after the westward departure of the <a href="/wiki/Lombards" title="Lombards">Lombards</a> (the last Germanic tribe) in 568, and under pressure from <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avars</a> established a <a href="/wiki/Slavic_settlement_of_the_Eastern_Alps" title="Slavic settlement of the Eastern Alps">Slavic settlement in the Eastern Alps</a>. From 623 to 624 or possibly 626 onwards, <a href="/wiki/Samo" title="Samo">King Samo</a> united the Alpine and Western Slavs against the Avars and Germanic peoples and established what is referred to as Samo's Kingdom. After its disintegration following Samo's death in 658 or 659, the ancestors of the <a href="/wiki/Slovenes" title="Slovenes">Slovenes</a> located in present-day <a href="/wiki/Carinthia_(state)" class="mw-redirect" title="Carinthia (state)">Carinthia</a> formed the independent <a href="/wiki/Carantania" title="Carantania">duchy of Carantania</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniola</a>, later duchy Carniola. Other parts of present-day Slovenia were again ruled by Avars before <a href="/wiki/Charlemagne" title="Charlemagne">Charlemagne</a>'s victory over them in 803.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Middle_Ages">Middle Ages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Middle Ages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg/220px-Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg/330px-Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg/440px-Kaernten_herzogeinsetzung.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="740" /></a><figcaption>A depiction of an ancient democratic ritual of Slovene-speaking tribes, which took place on the <a href="/wiki/Prince%27s_Stone" title="Prince&#39;s Stone">Prince's Stone</a> in <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a> until 1414</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Carantanians" title="Carantanians">Carantanians</a>, one of the ancestral groups of the modern Slovenes, particularly the <a href="/wiki/Carinthian_Slovenes" title="Carinthian Slovenes">Carinthian Slovenes</a>, were the first Slavic people to <a href="/wiki/Christianization_of_the_Slavs" title="Christianization of the Slavs">accept Christianity</a>. They were mostly Christianized by Irish missionaries, among them <a href="/wiki/Modestus_(Apostle_of_Carantania)" title="Modestus (Apostle of Carantania)">Modestus</a>, known as the "Apostle of Carantanians". This process, together with the Christianization of the <a href="/wiki/Bavarians" title="Bavarians">Bavarians</a>, was later described in the memorandum known as the <a href="/wiki/Conversio_Bagoariorum_et_Carantanorum" title="Conversio Bagoariorum et Carantanorum">Conversio Bagoariorum et Carantanorum</a>, which is thought to have overemphasized the role of the Church of Salzburg in the Christianization process over similar efforts of the Patriarchate of <a href="/wiki/Aquileia" title="Aquileia">Aquileia</a>.
</p><p>In the mid-8th century, Carantania became a vassal duchy under the rule of the <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavarians</a>, who began <a href="/wiki/Christianization" title="Christianization">spreading Christianity</a>. Three decades later, the <a href="/wiki/Carantanians" title="Carantanians">Carantanians</a> were incorporated, together with the Bavarians, into the <a href="/wiki/Carolingian_Empire" title="Carolingian Empire">Carolingian Empire</a>. During the same period <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniola</a>, too, came under the Franks, and was Christianised from <a href="/wiki/Aquileia" title="Aquileia">Aquileia</a>. Following the anti-Frankish rebellion of <a href="/wiki/Liudewit" class="mw-redirect" title="Liudewit">Liudewit</a> at the beginning of the 9th century, the <a href="/wiki/Franks" title="Franks">Franks</a> removed the Carantanian princes, replacing them with their own border dukes. Consequently, the Frankish <a href="/wiki/Feudal_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Feudal system">feudal system</a> reached the Slovene territory.
</p><p>After the victory of Emperor <a href="/wiki/Otto_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Otto I">Otto I</a> over the <a href="/wiki/Magyars" class="mw-redirect" title="Magyars">Magyars</a> in 955, Slovene territory was divided into a number of border regions of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>. Carantania was elevated into the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Carinthia" title="Duchy of Carinthia">Duchy of Carinthia</a> in 976.
</p><p>By the 11th century, the Germanization of what is now <a href="/wiki/Lower_Austria" title="Lower Austria">Lower Austria</a>, effectively isolated the Slovene-inhabited territory from the other <a href="/wiki/Western_Slavs" class="mw-redirect" title="Western Slavs">western Slavs</a>, speeding up the development of the <a href="/wiki/Carantanians" title="Carantanians">Slavs of Carantania</a> and of <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniola</a> into an independent Carantanian/Carniolans/Slovene ethnic group. By the high Middle Ages, the historic provinces of Carniola, <a href="/wiki/Styria_(duchy)" class="mw-redirect" title="Styria (duchy)">Styria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carinthia_(duchy)" class="mw-redirect" title="Carinthia (duchy)">Carinthia</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_of_Gorizia" title="County of Gorizia">Gorizia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trieste" title="Trieste">Trieste</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Istria" title="Istria">Istria</a> developed from the border regions and were incorporated into the medieval <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>. The consolidation and formation of these historical lands took place in a long period between the 11th and 14th centuries, and were led by a number of important feudal families, such as the <a href="/wiki/Bernhard_von_Spanheim" title="Bernhard von Spanheim">Dukes of Spanheim</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Meinhardiner" class="mw-redirect" title="Meinhardiner">Counts of Gorizia</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Counts_of_Celje" title="Counts of Celje">Counts of Celje</a>, and, finally, the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg" title="House of Habsburg">House of Habsburg</a>. In a parallel process, an intensive Germanization significantly diminished the extent of Slovene-speaking areas. By the 15th century, the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Lands" title="Slovene Lands">Slovene ethnic territory</a> was reduced to its present size.<sup id="cite_ref-culture.si_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture.si-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1335, <a href="/wiki/Henry_of_Bohemia" title="Henry of Bohemia">Henry of Gorizia</a>, Duke of Carinthia, Landgrave of Carniola and Count of Tyrol died without a male heir, his daughter <a href="/wiki/Margaret,_Countess_of_Tyrol" title="Margaret, Countess of Tyrol">Margaret</a> was able to keep the <a href="/wiki/County_of_Tyrol" title="County of Tyrol">County of Tyrol</a>, while the Wittelsbach emperor <a href="/wiki/Louis_IV,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Louis IV, Holy Roman Emperor">Louis IV</a> passed Carinthia and Carniolan march to the Habsburg duke <a href="/wiki/Albert_II,_Duke_of_Austria" title="Albert II, Duke of Austria">Albert II of Austria</a>, whose mother, <a href="/wiki/Elisabeth_of_Carinthia,_Queen_of_the_Romans" title="Elisabeth of Carinthia, Queen of the Romans">Elisabeth of Carinthia</a> is a sister of the late duke <a href="/wiki/Henry_of_Bohemia" title="Henry of Bohemia">Henry of Gorizia</a>. Therefore, most of the territory of present-day Slovenia became a hereditary land of the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy" title="Habsburg monarchy">Habsburg monarchy</a>. As with the other component parts of the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy" title="Habsburg monarchy">Habsburg monarchy</a>, Carinthia and Carniola remained a semi-autonomous state with its own constitutional structure for a long time. The <a href="/wiki/Counts_of_Celje" title="Counts of Celje">counts of Celje</a>, a feudal family from this area who in 1436 acquired the title of state princes, were <a href="/wiki/Habsburgs" class="mw-redirect" title="Habsburgs">Habsburgs</a>' powerful competitors for some time. This large dynasty, important at a European political level, had its seat in Slovene territory but died out in 1456. Its numerous large estates subsequently became the property of the Habsburgs, who retained control of the area right up until the beginning of the 20th century. <a href="/wiki/Patria_del_Friuli" title="Patria del Friuli">Patria del Friuli</a> ruled present western Slovenia until <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Venetian</a> takeover in 1420.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg/220px-Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="173" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg/330px-Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg/440px-Boj_s_Turki-Valvasor.jpg 2x" data-file-width="974" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman</a> army <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Habsburg_wars" title="Ottoman–Habsburg wars">battling</a> the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy" title="Habsburg monarchy">Habsburgs</a> in present-day Slovenia during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Turkish_War" title="Great Turkish War">Great Turkish War</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>At the end of the Middle Ages, the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Lands" title="Slovene Lands">Slovene Lands</a> suffered a serious economic and demographic setback because of the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Habsburg_wars" title="Ottoman–Habsburg wars">Turkish raids</a>. In 1515, a <a href="/wiki/Slovene_peasant_revolt_of_1515" title="Slovene peasant revolt of 1515">peasant revolt</a> spread across nearly the whole Slovene territory. In 1572 and 1573 the <a href="/wiki/Croatian-Slovenian_peasant_revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="Croatian-Slovenian peasant revolt">Croatian-Slovenian peasant revolt</a> wrought havoc throughout the wider region. Such uprisings, which often met with bloody defeats, continued throughout the 17th century.<sup id="cite_ref-culture.si_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture.si-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_modern_period">Early modern period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Early modern period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After the dissolution of the Republic of Venice in 1797, the Venetian Slovenia was passed to the Austrian Empire. The <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Lands" title="Slovene Lands">Slovene Lands</a> were part of the French-administered <a href="/wiki/Illyrian_Provinces" title="Illyrian Provinces">Illyrian Provinces</a> established by Napoleon, the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian Empire</a> and <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>. Slovenes inhabited most of <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniola</a>, the southern part of the duchies of <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Carinthia" title="Duchy of Carinthia">Carinthia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Styria" title="Duchy of Styria">Styria</a>, the northern and eastern areas of the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Littoral" title="Austrian Littoral">Austrian Littoral</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Lands_of_the_Crown_of_Saint_Stephen" title="Lands of the Crown of Saint Stephen">Kingdom of Hungary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Industrialization was accompanied by construction of railroads to link cities and markets, but the urbanization was limited.
</p><p>Due to limited opportunities, between 1880 and 1910 there was extensive emigration; around 300,000 Slovenes (1 in 6) emigrated to other countries,<sup id="cite_ref-Benderly9_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Benderly9-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> mostly <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Americans" title="Slovene Americans">to the US</a>, but also <a href="/wiki/Slovene_communities_in_South_America" title="Slovene communities in South America">to South America</a> (the main part to <a href="/wiki/Argentina" title="Argentina">Argentina</a>), Germany, <a href="/wiki/Egypt" title="Egypt">Egypt</a>, and to larger cities in Austria-Hungary, especially <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> and <a href="/wiki/Graz" title="Graz">Graz</a>. Despite this emigration, the population of Slovenia increased significantly.<sup id="cite_ref-Benderly9_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Benderly9-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Literacy was exceptionally high, at 80–90%.<sup id="cite_ref-Benderly9_48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Benderly9-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 19th century also saw a revival of culture in <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a>, accompanied by a <a href="/wiki/Romantic_nationalism" title="Romantic nationalism">Romantic nationalist</a> quest for cultural and political autonomy. The idea of a <a href="/wiki/United_Slovenia" title="United Slovenia">United Slovenia</a>, first advanced during the <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1848" title="Revolutions of 1848">revolutions of 1848</a>, became the common platform of most Slovenian parties and political movements in Austria-Hungary. During the same period, <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavism" title="Yugoslavism">Yugoslavism</a>, an ideology stressing the unity of all <a href="/wiki/South_Slavs" title="South Slavs">South Slavic peoples</a>, spread as a reaction to <a href="/wiki/Pan-Germanism" title="Pan-Germanism">Pan-German nationalism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Italian_irredentism" title="Italian irredentism">Italian irredentism</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_I">World War I</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: World War I">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Italian_front_(World_War_I)" title="Italian front (World War I)">Italian front (World War I)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_the_Isonzo" title="Battles of the Isonzo">Battles of the Isonzo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_London_(1915)" title="Treaty of London (1915)">Treaty of London (1915)</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Corfu_Declaration" title="Corfu Declaration">Corfu Declaration</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg/220px-Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg/330px-Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg/440px-Eingebaute_Mannschaftsunterst%C3%A4nde_am_Monte_Sabotino.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1680" data-file-height="1198" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_the_Isonzo" title="Battles of the Isonzo">Battles of the Isonzo</a> took place mostly in rugged mountainous areas above the Soča River.</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a> brought heavy casualties to Slovenes, particularly the twelve <a href="/wiki/Battles_of_the_Isonzo" title="Battles of the Isonzo">Battles of the Isonzo</a>, which took place in present-day Slovenia's western border area with Italy. Hundreds of thousands of Slovene conscripts were drafted into the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Army" title="Austro-Hungarian Army">Austro-Hungarian Army</a>, and over 30,000 of them died. Hundreds of thousands of Slovenes from <a href="/wiki/Princely_County_of_Gorizia_and_Gradisca" title="Princely County of Gorizia and Gradisca">Princely County of Gorizia and Gradisca</a> were resettled in <a href="/wiki/Refugee_camp" title="Refugee camp">refugee camps</a> in Italy and Austria. While the refugees in Austria received decent treatment, the Slovene refugees in Italian camps were treated as state enemies, and several thousand died of malnutrition and diseases between 1915 and 1918.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Entire areas of the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a> were destroyed.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Rapallo_(1920)" title="Treaty of Rapallo (1920)">Treaty of Rapallo</a> of 1920 left approximately 327,000 out of the total population of 1.3&#160;million Slovenes in Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-SacroEgoismo2012_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SacroEgoismo2012-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Cresciani_ClashOfCivilisations_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cresciani_ClashOfCivilisations-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/March_on_Rome" title="March on Rome">fascists took power</a> in Italy, they were subjected to a policy of violent Fascist <a href="/wiki/Italianization" title="Italianization">Italianization</a>. This caused the mass emigration of Slovenes, especially the middle class, from the Slovene Littoral and <a href="/wiki/Trieste" title="Trieste">Trieste</a> to <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia" title="Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a> and South America. Those who remained organized several connected networks of both passive and armed resistance. The best known was the <a href="/wiki/Anti-fascism" title="Anti-fascism">militant anti-fascist</a> organization <a href="/wiki/TIGR" title="TIGR">TIGR</a>, formed in 1927 to fight Fascist oppression of the Slovene and Croat populations in the <a href="/wiki/Julian_March" title="Julian March">Julian March</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mira_Cencič_1997_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mira_Cencič_1997-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Tatjana_Rejec_1995_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tatjana_Rejec_1995-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Kingdom_of_Serbs.2C_Croats.2C_and_Slovenes_.28later_the_Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Kingdom_of_Serbs,_Croats,_and_Slovenes_(later_the_Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia)">Kingdom of Serbs, Croats, and Slovenes (later the Kingdom of Yugoslavia)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Kingdom of Serbs, Croats, and Slovenes (later the Kingdom of Yugoslavia)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Creation_of_Yugoslavia" title="Creation of Yugoslavia">Creation of Yugoslavia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carinthian_Plebiscite" class="mw-redirect" title="Carinthian Plebiscite">Carinthian Plebiscite</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_Maister" title="Rudolf Maister">Rudolf Maister</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:KongressfallofAH.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/KongressfallofAH.jpg/220px-KongressfallofAH.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/KongressfallofAH.jpg/330px-KongressfallofAH.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/KongressfallofAH.jpg/440px-KongressfallofAH.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3140" data-file-height="2067" /></a><figcaption>The proclamation of the State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs at <a href="/wiki/Congress_Square" title="Congress Square">Congress Square</a> in Ljubljana on 20 October 1918</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Slovene_People%27s_Party_(historical)" title="Slovene People&#39;s Party (historical)">Slovene People's Party</a> launched a movement for self-determination, demanding the creation of a semi-independent <a href="/wiki/South_Slavic_peoples" class="mw-redirect" title="South Slavic peoples">South Slavic</a> state under <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Habsburg dynasty">Habsburg</a> rule. The proposal was picked up by most Slovene parties, and a mass mobilization of Slovene civil society, known as the <a href="/w/index.php?title=May_Declaration_(1917)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="May Declaration (1917) (page does not exist)">Declaration Movement</a>, followed.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> This demand was rejected by the Austrian political elites; but following the dissolution of the Austro-Hungarian Empire in the aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/First_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="First World War">First World War</a>, the <a href="/wiki/National_Council_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs" class="mw-redirect" title="National Council of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs">National Council of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs</a> took power in <a href="/wiki/Zagreb" title="Zagreb">Zagreb</a> on 6 October 1918. On 29 October, independence was declared by a national gathering in Ljubljana, and by the Croatian parliament, declaring the establishment of the new <a href="/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats,_and_Serbs" class="mw-redirect" title="State of Slovenes, Croats, and Serbs">State of Slovenes, Croats, and Serbs</a>.
</p><p>On 1 December 1918, the State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs merged with <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>, becoming part of the new <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbs,_Croats,_and_Slovenes" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Serbs, Croats, and Slovenes">Kingdom of Serbs, Croats, and Slovenes</a>; in 1929 it was renamed the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia" title="Kingdom of Yugoslavia">Kingdom of Yugoslavia</a>. The main territory of Slovenia, being the most industrialized and westernized compared to other less developed parts of Yugoslavia, became the main centre of industrial production: Compared to Serbia, for example, Slovenian industrial production was four times greater; and it was 22 times greater than in <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a>. The interwar period brought further industrialization in Slovenia, with rapid economic growth in the 1920s, followed by a relatively successful economic adjustment to the <a href="/wiki/1929_economic_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="1929 economic crisis">1929 economic crisis</a> and <a href="/wiki/Great_Depression" title="Great Depression">Great Depression</a>.
</p><p>Following a <a href="/wiki/Carinthian_Plebiscite" class="mw-redirect" title="Carinthian Plebiscite">plebiscite</a> in October 1920, the Slovene-speaking southern <a href="/wiki/Carinthia_(state)" class="mw-redirect" title="Carinthia (state)">Carinthia</a> was ceded to <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a>. With the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Trianon" title="Treaty of Trianon">Treaty of Trianon</a>, the Kingdom of Yugoslavia was awarded the Slovene-inhabited <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a> region, formerly part of Austria-Hungary. Slovenes living in territories that fell under the rule of the neighboring states—Italy, Austria, and Hungary—were subjected to <a href="/wiki/Cultural_assimilation" title="Cultural assimilation">assimilation</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II">World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/World_War_II_in_the_Slovene_Lands" title="World War II in the Slovene Lands">World War II in the Slovene Lands</a></div>
<table class="infobox" style="width: 90px;">
<tbody><tr>
<td><div role="img" class="noresize" style="width: 350px; line-height: 1; text-align: center; background-color: #ffffff; position: relative;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg/350px-Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg" decoding="async" width="350" height="241" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg/525px-Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg/700px-Provincia_di_Lubiana1941-1943.jpg 2x" data-file-width="976" data-file-height="672" /></a></span>
<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:114.75px; top:28.75px"><a href="/wiki/Austria_under_National_Socialism" class="mw-redirect" title="Austria under National Socialism"><span style="color:#0645AD;">Nazi Germany</span></a></div>
<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:308.65px; top:12.3px"><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1920%E2%80%9346)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1920–46)"><span style="color:#0645AD;">Hungary</span></a></div>
<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:1.35px; top:154.75px"><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy"><span style="color:#0645AD;">Fascist<br /><br />Italy</span></a></div>
<div style="position:absolute; font-size:85%; left:279.25px; top:150.9px"><a href="/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia" title="Independent State of Croatia"><span style="color:#0645AD;">Independent<br /><br />State of<br /><br />Croatia</span></a></div>
</div>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>During World War II, <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1920%E2%80%931946)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1920–1946)">Hungary</a> annexed northern areas (brown and dark green areas, respectively), while Fascist <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italy</a> annexed the vertically hashed black area (solid black western part having been annexed by Italy in 1920 with the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Rapallo_(1920)" title="Treaty of Rapallo (1920)">Treaty of Rapallo</a>). Some villages were incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia" title="Independent State of Croatia">Independent State of Croatia</a>. After 1943, Germany took over the Italian occupational area, as well.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Slovenia was the only present-day European nation that was trisected and completely annexed into both <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a> and Fascist <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italy</a> during World War II.<sup id="cite_ref-GJK_2013_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GJK_2013-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a> region in the east was annexed to Hungary, and some villages in the <a href="/wiki/Lower_Sava_Valley" title="Lower Sava Valley">Lower Sava Valley</a> were incorporated in the newly created Nazi puppet <a href="/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia" title="Independent State of Croatia">Independent State of Croatia</a> (NDH). Axis forces <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Yugoslavia" title="Invasion of Yugoslavia">invaded Yugoslavia</a> in April 1941 and defeated the country in a few weeks. The southern part, including <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a>, was annexed to Italy, while the Nazis took over the northern and eastern parts of the country. The Nazis had a plan of <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing" title="Ethnic cleansing">ethnic cleansing</a> of these areas,<sup id="cite_ref-HF2006_BerghahnBooks_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HF2006_BerghahnBooks-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> and they resettled or expelled the local Slovene civilian population to the puppet states of <a href="/wiki/Nedi%C4%87%27s_Serbia" class="mw-redirect" title="Nedić&#39;s Serbia">Nedić's Serbia</a> (7,500) and <a href="/wiki/NDH" class="mw-redirect" title="NDH">NDH</a> (10,000). In addition, some 46,000 Slovenes were expelled to Germany, including children who were separated from their parents and allocated to German families.<sup id="cite_ref-LM2009_UNC_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LM2009_UNC-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> At the same time, the ethnic Germans in the <a href="/wiki/Gottschee" title="Gottschee">Gottschee</a> enclave in the Italian annexation zone were resettled to the Nazi-controlled areas cleansed of their Slovene population.<sup id="cite_ref-L1993_UNC_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-L1993_UNC-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
Around 30,000 to 40,000 Slovene men were drafted to the <a href="/wiki/Wehrmacht" title="Wehrmacht">German Army</a> and sent to the Eastern front. Slovene was banned from education, and its use in public life was limited.<sup id="cite_ref-GJK_2013_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GJK_2013-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In south-central Slovenia, annexed by Fascist Italy and renamed the <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Ljubljana" title="Province of Ljubljana">Province of Ljubljana</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Front_of_the_Slovene_Nation" title="Liberation Front of the Slovene Nation">Slovenian National Liberation Front</a> was organized in April 1941. Led by the Communist Party, it formed the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Partisans" title="Slovene Partisans">Slovene Partisan</a> units as part of the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_Partisans" title="Yugoslav Partisans">Yugoslav Partisans</a> led by the Communist leader <a href="/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito" title="Josip Broz Tito">Josip Broz Tito</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-JJR2013_OxfordPress_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JJR2013_OxfordPress-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AS_2005_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AS_2005-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the resistance started in summer 1941, Italian violence against the Slovene civilian population escalated. The Italian authorities deported some 25,000 people to <a href="/wiki/Italian_concentration_camps" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian concentration camps">concentration camps</a>, which equaled 7.5% of the population of their occupation zone. The most infamous ones were <a href="/wiki/Rab_concentration_camp" title="Rab concentration camp">Rab</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gonars_concentration_camp" title="Gonars concentration camp">Gonars</a>. To counter the Communist-led insurgence, the Italians sponsored local anti-guerrilla units, formed mostly by the local conservative Catholic Slovene population that resented the revolutionary violence of the partisans. After the <a href="/wiki/Italian_armistice" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian armistice">Italian armistice</a> of September 1943, the Germans took over both the Province of Ljubljana and the Slovenian Littoral, incorporating them into what was known as the <a href="/wiki/Operation_Zone_of_Adriatic_Coastal_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Operation Zone of Adriatic Coastal Region">Operation Zone of Adriatic Coastal Region</a>. They united the Slovene anti-Communist counter-insurgence into the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Home_Guard" title="Slovene Home Guard">Slovene Home Guard</a> and appointed a puppet regime in the Province of Ljubljana. The anti-Nazi resistance however expanded, creating its own administrative structures as the basis for Slovene statehood within a new, federal and socialist Yugoslavia.<sup id="cite_ref-Tomasevich_2001_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tomasevich_2001-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Dennison_I._Rusinow_1978,_p._2_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dennison_I._Rusinow_1978,_p._2-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1945, <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia" title="Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a> was liberated by the partisan resistance and soon became a socialist federation known as the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">People's Federal Republic of Yugoslavia</a>. <a class="mw-selflink-fragment" href="#Etymology">The first Slovenian republic</a>, named <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Slovenia" title="Socialist Republic of Slovenia">Federal Slovenia</a>, was a constituent republic of the Yugoslavian federation, led by its own pro-Communist leadership.
</p><p>Approximately 8% of the Slovene population died during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>. The small Jewish community, mostly in the <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a> region, perished in 1944 in the <a href="/wiki/Holocaust_in_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust in Hungary">holocaust of Hungarian Jews</a>. The German-speaking minority, amounting to 2.5% of the Slovenian population prior to the war, was either expelled or killed in the aftermath of the war. Hundreds of <a href="/wiki/Istrian_Italians" title="Istrian Italians">Istrian Italians</a> and Slovenes that opposed communism were killed in the <a href="/wiki/Foibe_killings" class="mw-redirect" title="Foibe killings">foibe massacres</a>, and more than 25,000 fled or were expelled from <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Istria" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian Istria">Slovenian Istria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Around 130,000 persons, mostly political and military opponents, were executed in May and June 1945.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Socialist_period">Socialist period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Socialist period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During the re-establishment of Yugoslavia in World War II, the <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Slovenia" title="Socialist Republic of Slovenia">first Slovenian republic, Federal Slovenia</a>, was created and it became part of <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" title="Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Federal Yugoslavia</a>. It was a <a href="/wiki/Socialist_state" title="Socialist state">socialist state</a>, but because of the <a href="/wiki/Tito%E2%80%93Stalin_split" title="Tito–Stalin split">Tito–Stalin split</a> in 1948, economic and personal freedoms were much broader than in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc">Eastern Bloc</a> countries. In 1947, the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a> and the western half of <a href="/wiki/Inner_Carniola" title="Inner Carniola">Inner Carniola</a>, which had been annexed by Italy after World War One, were annexed to Slovenia. 
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png/220px-Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="193" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png/330px-Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png/440px-Economy_Yugoslavia_1975.png 2x" data-file-width="941" data-file-height="824" /></a><figcaption>Average strength of Yugoslav economy as a deviation from the main (Yugoslavia = 100&#160;%) indicator 1975. <a href="/wiki/SR_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="SR Slovenia">SR Slovenia</a> (dark green) was, along with <a href="/wiki/SR_Croatia" class="mw-redirect" title="SR Croatia">SR Croatia</a> and <a href="/wiki/SAP_Vojvodina" class="mw-redirect" title="SAP Vojvodina">SAP Vojvodina</a> (light green), the richest entity of <a href="/wiki/SFR_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="SFR Yugoslavia">SFR Yugoslavia</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>After the failure of <a href="/wiki/Collective_farming" title="Collective farming">forced collectivisation</a> that was attempted from 1949 to 1953, a policy of gradual economic liberalisation, known as <a href="/wiki/Workers%27_self-management" title="Workers&#39; self-management">workers self-management</a>, was introduced under the advice and supervision of the Slovene Marxist theoretician and Communist leader <a href="/wiki/Edvard_Kardelj" title="Edvard Kardelj">Edvard Kardelj</a>, the main ideologue of the <a href="/wiki/Titoism" title="Titoism">Titoist</a> path to socialism. Suspected opponents of this policy both from within and outside the Communist party were persecuted and thousands were sent to <a href="/wiki/Goli_otok" class="mw-redirect" title="Goli otok">Goli otok</a>.
</p><p>The late 1950s saw a policy of liberalization in the cultural sphere as well, and unlimited border crossing into western countries was allowed, both for Yugoslav citizens and for foreigners. In 1956, <a href="/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito" title="Josip Broz Tito">Josip Broz Tito</a>, together with other leaders, founded the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>. In the 1950s, Slovenia's economy developed rapidly and was strongly industrialized. With further economic decentralization of Yugoslavia in 1965–66, Slovenia's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">domestic product</a> was 2.5 times the average of Yugoslav republics. While a Communist country, after the Tito–Stalin split Yugoslavia initiated a period of military neutrality and non-alignment. JAT Yugoslav Airlines was the flag carrier and during its existence it grew to become one of the leading airlines in Europe both by fleet and destinations. By the 1970s more airlines were created including Slovenian <a href="/wiki/Adria_Airways" title="Adria Airways">Adria Airways</a> mostly focused in the growing tourist industry. Until the 1980s, <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Slovenia" title="Socialist Republic of Slovenia">Slovenia</a> enjoyed relatively broad autonomy within the federation. It was the most liberal communist state in Europe, the passport of Yugoslavia Federation allowed Yugoslavians to travel to most world countries of any socialist country during the Cold War. Many people worked in western countries, which reduced unemployment in their home country.
</p><p>Opposition to the regime was mostly limited to intellectual and literary circles and became especially vocal after Tito's death in 1980 when the economic and political situation in Yugoslavia became very strained.<sup id="cite_ref-culture.si_46-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture.si-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Political disputes around economic measures were echoed in the public sentiment, as many Slovenians felt they were being economically exploited, having to sustain an expensive and inefficient federal administration.
</p>
<h3><span id="Slovenian_Spring.2C_democracy_and_independence"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Slovenian_Spring,_democracy_and_independence">Slovenian Spring, democracy and independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Slovenian Spring, democracy and independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1987 a group of intellectuals demanded Slovene independence in the <a href="/wiki/Contributions_for_the_Slovenian_National_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="Contributions for the Slovenian National Program">57th edition</a> of the magazine <i><a href="/wiki/Nova_revija_(magazine)" title="Nova revija (magazine)">Nova revija</a></i>. Demands for democratisation and more Slovenian independence were sparked off. A mass democratic movement, coordinated by the <a href="/wiki/Committee_for_the_Defence_of_Human_Rights" title="Committee for the Defence of Human Rights">Committee for the Defence of Human Rights</a>, pushed the Communists in the direction of democratic reforms.
</p><p>In September 1989, numerous <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_amendment" title="Constitutional amendment">constitutional amendments</a> were passed to introduce <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_democracy" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliamentary democracy">parliamentary democracy</a> to Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>  On 7 March 1990, the Slovenian Assembly changed the official name of the state to the "Republic of Slovenia".<sup id="cite_ref-twenty.si_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-twenty.si-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> In April 1990, the first democratic election in Slovenia took place, and the united opposition movement <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Opposition_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Democratic Opposition of Slovenia">DEMOS</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Jo%C5%BEe_Pu%C4%8Dnik" title="Jože Pučnik">Jože Pučnik</a> emerged victorious.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg/220px-Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="121" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg/330px-Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg/440px-Teritorialci_so_z_armbrustom_zadeli_tank_v_kri%C5%BEi%C5%A1%C4%8Du_pred_MMP_Ro%C5%BEna_Dolina..jpg 2x" data-file-width="1158" data-file-height="636" /></a><figcaption>Slovenian Territorial Defense Units counterattacking the Yugoslav National Army tank who entered Slovenia during the <a href="/wiki/Ten-Day_War" title="Ten-Day War">Ten-Day War</a>, 1991</figcaption></figure>
<p>The initial revolutionary events in Slovenia pre-dated the <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989" title="Revolutions of 1989">Revolutions of 1989</a> in Eastern Europe by almost a year, but went largely unnoticed by international observers. On 23 December 1990, more than 88% of the electorate voted for a sovereign and independent Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-Felicijan_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Felicijan-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-STAT11_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-STAT11-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> On 25 June 1991, Slovenia became independent.<sup id="cite_ref-Škrk1999_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Škrk1999-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Jonsson_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jonsson-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> On 27 June in the early morning, the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_People%27s_Army" title="Yugoslav People&#39;s Army">Yugoslav People's Army</a> dispatched its forces to prevent further measures for the establishment of a new country, which led to the <a href="/wiki/Ten-Day_War" title="Ten-Day War">Ten-Day War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Race_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Race-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-SAF_History_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SAF_History-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> On 7 July, the <a href="/wiki/Brijuni_Agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="Brijuni Agreement">Brijuni Agreement</a> was signed, implementing a truce and a three-month halt of the enforcement of Slovenia's independence.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> At the end of the month, the last soldiers of the Yugoslav Army left Slovenia.
</p><p>In December 1991, a new <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Slovenia" title="Constitution of Slovenia">constitution</a> was adopted,<sup id="cite_ref-Jonsson_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jonsson-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> followed in 1992 by the laws on <a href="/wiki/Privatization" title="Privatization">denationalisation and privatization</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Klemenčič2004_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Klemenčič2004-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The members of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> recognised Slovenia as an independent state on 15 January 1992, and the United Nations accepted it as a member on 22 May 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-Borak2004_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Borak2004-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia joined the European Union on 1 May 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia has one Commissioner in the <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission">European Commission</a>, and seven Slovene parliamentarians were elected to the <a href="/wiki/European_Parliament" title="European Parliament">European Parliament</a> at elections on 13 June 2004. In 2004 Slovenia also joined <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia subsequently succeeded in meeting the <a href="/wiki/Maastricht_criteria" class="mw-redirect" title="Maastricht criteria">Maastricht criteria</a> and joined the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a> (the first transition country to do so) on 1 January 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> It was the first post-Communist country to hold the <a href="/wiki/Presidency_of_the_Council_of_the_European_Union" title="Presidency of the Council of the European Union">Presidency of the Council of the European Union</a>, for the first six months of 2008. On 21 July 2010, it became a member of the OECD.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The disillusionment with domestic socio-economic elites at municipal and national levels was expressed at the <a href="/wiki/2012%E2%80%932013_Slovenian_protests" title="2012–2013 Slovenian protests">2012–2013 Slovenian protests</a> on a wider scale than in the smaller <a href="/wiki/15_October_2011_global_protests#Slovenia" title="15 October 2011 global protests">15 October 2011 protests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> In relation to the leading politicians' response to allegations made by the official <a href="/wiki/Commission_for_the_Prevention_of_Corruption_of_the_Republic_of_Slovenia" title="Commission for the Prevention of Corruption of the Republic of Slovenia">Commission for the Prevention of Corruption of the Republic of Slovenia</a>, legal experts expressed the need for changes in the system that would limit political <a href="/wiki/Arbitrariness" title="Arbitrariness">arbitrariness</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:PCR" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:PCR"><span title="The material near this tag may lack sufficient context (January 2017)">needs context</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Slovenia" title="Geography of Slovenia">Geography of Slovenia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Protected areas of Slovenia">Protected areas of Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Slovenia is in <a href="/wiki/Central_Europe" title="Central Europe">Central</a> Europe touching the Alps and bordering the Mediterranean Sea. At the regional conference in Prague in 1994, the International Geographical Union placed Slovenia among the nine Central European countries.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> It lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/45th_parallel_north" title="45th parallel north">45°</a> and <a href="/wiki/47th_parallel_north" title="47th parallel north">47° N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/13th_meridian_east" title="13th meridian east">13°</a> and <a href="/wiki/17th_meridian_east" title="17th meridian east">17° E</a>. The <a href="/wiki/15th_meridian_east" title="15th meridian east">15th meridian east</a> almost corresponds to the middle line of the country in the direction west–east.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Geometric_Centre_of_the_Republic_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Geometric Centre of the Republic of Slovenia">Geometric Centre of the Republic of Slovenia</a> is located at <a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">coordinates</a> 46°07'11.8" N and 14°48'55.2" E.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> It lies in <a href="/wiki/Slivna" title="Slivna">Slivna</a> in the Municipality of <a href="/wiki/Litija" title="Litija">Litija</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia's highest peak is <a href="/wiki/Triglav" title="Triglav">Triglav</a> (2,864&#160;m or 9,396&#160;ft); the country's average height <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above sea level</a> is 557&#160;m (1,827&#160;ft).
</p><p>Four major European geographic regions meet in Slovenia: the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Alps" title="Dinaric Alps">Dinarides</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Pannonian Plain">Pannonian Plain</a>, and the Mediterranean Sea. Although on the shore of the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a> near the Mediterranean Sea, most of Slovenia is in the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">drainage basin</a>. The Alps—including the <a href="/wiki/Julian_Alps" title="Julian Alps">Julian Alps</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kamnik-Savinja_Alps" class="mw-redirect" title="Kamnik-Savinja Alps">Kamnik-Savinja Alps</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Karawanks" title="Karawanks">Karawank</a> chain, as well as the <a href="/wiki/Pohorje" title="Pohorje">Pohorje</a> massif—dominate Northern Slovenia along its long border with <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a>. Slovenia's Adriatic coastline stretches approximately 47 kilometres (29&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> from Italy to Croatia.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mangart_16.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Mangart_16.jpg/220px-Mangart_16.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Mangart_16.jpg/330px-Mangart_16.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Mangart_16.jpg/440px-Mangart_16.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4200" data-file-height="2800" /></a><figcaption>Mount <a href="/wiki/Mangart" title="Mangart">Mangart</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/Julian_Alps" title="Julian Alps">Julian Alps</a>, is the third-highest peak in Slovenia, after <a href="/wiki/Triglav" title="Triglav">Triglav</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0krlatica" title="Škrlatica">Škrlatica</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The term "<a href="/wiki/Karst_topography" class="mw-redirect" title="Karst topography">Karst topography</a>" refers to that of southwestern Slovenia's <a href="/wiki/Karst_Plateau" title="Karst Plateau">Karst Plateau</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Limestone" title="Limestone">limestone</a> region of underground rivers, gorges, and caves, between Ljubljana and the Mediterranean Sea. On the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Pannonian plain">Pannonian plain</a> to the East and Northeast, toward the Croatian and Hungarian borders, the landscape is essentially flat. However, most of Slovenia is hilly or mountainous, with around 90% of its land surface 200&#160;m (656&#160;ft) or more <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above sea level</a>.
</p><p>More than half of Slovenia, which is 11,823&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 4,565&#160;sq&#160;mi, is forested;<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> ranking it third in Europe, <a href="/w/index.php?title=European_countries_by_forest_area&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="European countries by forest area (page does not exist)">by percentage of area forested</a>, after <a href="/wiki/Finland" title="Finland">Finland</a> and Sweden. The areas are covered mostly by <a href="/wiki/Beech" title="Beech">beech</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fir" title="Fir">fir</a>-beech and beech-<a href="/wiki/Oak" title="Oak">oak</a> forests and have a relatively high production capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Remnants of primeval forests are still to be found, the largest in the <a href="/wiki/Ko%C4%8Devje" title="Kočevje">Kočevje</a> area. Grassland covers 5,593&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (2,159&#160;sq&#160;mi) and fields and gardens (954&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 368&#160;sq&#160;mi). There are 363&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (140&#160;sq&#160;mi) of orchards and 216&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (83&#160;sq&#160;mi) of vineyards.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Geology">Geology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Geology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg/220px-%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg/330px-%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg/440px-%C5%BDlebi%C4%8Di.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="683" /></a><figcaption>Solution runnels (also known as rillenkarren) are a <a href="/wiki/Karst_topography" class="mw-redirect" title="Karst topography">karst</a> feature on the <a href="/wiki/Karst_Plateau" title="Karst Plateau">Karst Plateau</a>, as in many other karst areas of the world.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenia is in a rather active <a href="/wiki/Seismic_zone" title="Seismic zone">seismic zone</a> because of its position on the small <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Plate" title="Adriatic Plate">Adriatic Plate</a>, which is squeezed between the <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_Plate" title="Eurasian Plate">Eurasian Plate</a> to the north and the <a href="/wiki/African_Plate" title="African Plate">African Plate</a> to the south and rotates counter-clockwise.<sup id="cite_ref-Seisme_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seisme-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Thus the country is at the junction of three important <a href="/wiki/Tectonics" title="Tectonics">geotectonic</a> units: the Alps to the north, the Dinaric Alps to the south and the Pannonian Basin to the east.<sup id="cite_ref-Seisme_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seisme-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Scientists have been able to identify 60 destructive earthquakes in the past. Additionally, a network of seismic stations is active throughout the country.<sup id="cite_ref-Seisme_90-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seisme-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many parts of Slovenia have a <a href="/wiki/Carbonate_rock" title="Carbonate rock">carbonate</a> <a href="/wiki/Bedrock" title="Bedrock">bedrock</a> and extensive <a href="/wiki/Cave_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Cave system">cave systems</a> have developed.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Natural_regions">Natural regions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Natural regions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
