<p>The first regionalisations of Slovenia were made by geographers <a href="/wiki/Anton_Melik" title="Anton Melik">Anton Melik</a> (1935–1936) and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Svetozar_Ile%C5%A1i%C4%8D&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Svetozar Ilešič (page does not exist)">Svetozar Ilešič</a> (1968). The newer regionalisation by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivan_Gams&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ivan Gams (page does not exist)">Ivan Gams</a> divided Slovenia in the following macroregions:<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Strunjan_bay_(24209577441).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg/220px-Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg/330px-Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg/440px-Strunjan_bay_%2824209577441%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4928" data-file-height="3264" /></a><figcaption>Slovenian coast with cliffs</figcaption></figure>
<ul><li>the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a> (<i>Alpe</i>)</li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Prealps" title="Slovene Prealps">subalpine landscapes</a> (<i>predalpski svet</i>)</li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a> or <a href="/wiki/Submediterranean" class="mw-redirect" title="Submediterranean">Submediterranean</a> Slovenia (<i>Primorje</i> or <i>submediteranska Slovenija</i>)</li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Alps" title="Dinaric Alps">Dinaric</a> <a href="/wiki/Plateaus" class="mw-redirect" title="Plateaus">plateaus</a> of the continental Slovenia (<i>dinarske planote celinske Slovenije</i>)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Pannonian Plain">Subpannonian Slovenia</a> (<i>subpanonska Slovenija</i>)</li></ul>
<p>According to a newer natural geographic regionalisation, the country consists of four <a href="/wiki/Macroregion" title="Macroregion">macroregions</a>. These are the Alpine, the Mediterranean, the Dinaric, and the <a href="/wiki/Pannonia" title="Pannonia">Pannonian</a> landscapes. Macroregions are defined according to major relief units (the Alps, the Pannonian plain, the Dinaric mountains) and climate types (submediterranean, temperate continental, mountain climate).<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> These are often quite interwoven.
</p><p>Protected areas of Slovenia include national parks, regional parks, and nature parks, the largest of which is <a href="/wiki/Triglav_National_Park" title="Triglav National Park">Triglav National Park</a>. There are 286 <a href="/wiki/Natura_2000" title="Natura 2000">Natura 2000</a> designated protected areas, which include 36% of the country's land area, the largest percentage among European Union states.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, according to <a href="/wiki/Yale_University" title="Yale University">Yale University</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Environmental_Performance_Index" title="Environmental Performance Index">Environmental Performance Index</a>, Slovenia is considered a "strong performer" in environmental protection efforts.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG/330px-Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG" decoding="async" width="330" height="485" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG/495px-Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG/660px-Climate_types_Slovenia_1970-2000.JPG 2x" data-file-width="765" data-file-height="1125" /></a><figcaption>Climate types of Slovenia 1970–2000 and <a href="/wiki/Climograph" title="Climograph">climographs</a> for selected settlements</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenia is located in temperate latitudes. The climate is also influenced by the variety of relief, and the influence of the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a>. In the northeast, the <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">continental climate</a> type with greatest difference between winter and summer temperatures prevails. In the coastal region, there is sub-<a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_climate" title="Mediterranean climate">Mediterranean climate</a>. The effect of the sea on the temperature rates is also visible up the <a href="/wiki/So%C4%8Da" title="Soča">Soča</a> Valley, while a severe <a href="/wiki/Alpine_climate" title="Alpine climate">Alpine climate</a> is present in the high mountain regions. There is a strong interaction between these three climatic systems across most of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-arso.gov.si_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-arso.gov.si-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-arso.gov.si_Meteo_Archive_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-arso.gov.si_Meteo_Archive-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Precipitation_(meteorology)" class="mw-redirect" title="Precipitation (meteorology)">Precipitation</a>, often coming from the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Genoa" title="Gulf of Genoa">Gulf of Genoa</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> varies across the country as well, with over 3,500&#160;mm (138&#160;in) in some western regions and dropping down to 800&#160;mm (31&#160;in) in <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a>. Snow is quite frequent in winter and the record snow cover in Ljubljana was recorded in 1952 at 146&#160;cm (57&#160;in).
</p><p>Compared to Western Europe, Slovenia is not very windy, because it lies in the slipstream of the Alps. The average wind speeds are lower than in the plains of the nearby countries. Due to the rugged terrain, local vertical winds with daily periods are present. Besides these, there are three winds of particular regional importance: the <a href="/wiki/Bora_(wind)" title="Bora (wind)">bora</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Jugo" class="mw-redirect" title="Jugo">jugo</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Foehn_wind" title="Foehn wind">foehn</a>. The jugo and the bora are characteristic of the Littoral. Whereas the jugo is humid and warm, the bora is usually cold and gusty. The foehn is typical of the Alpine regions in the north of Slovenia. Generally present in Slovenia are the northeast wind, the southeast wind and the <a href="/wiki/North_wind" title="North wind">north wind</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Waters">Waters</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Waters">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lake_Bohinj_(9452094709).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg/220px-Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="104" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg/330px-Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg/440px-Lake_Bohinj_%289452094709%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="483" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Lake_Bohinj" title="Lake Bohinj">Lake Bohinj</a>, largest Slovenian lake, one of the two springs of the <a href="/wiki/Sava" title="Sava">Sava</a> River</figcaption></figure>
<p>The territory of Slovenia mainly (16,423 square kilometers or 6,341 square miles, i.e. 81%) belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> basin, and a smaller part (3,850 square kilometers or 1,490 square miles, i.e. 19%) belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a> basin. These two parts are divided into smaller units in regard to their central rivers, the <a href="/wiki/Mur_(river)" title="Mur (river)">Mura</a> River basin, the <a href="/wiki/Drava" title="Drava">Drava</a> River basin, the <a href="/wiki/Sava" title="Sava">Sava</a> River basin with <a href="/wiki/Kupa" title="Kupa">Kolpa</a> River basin, and the basin of the Adriatic rivers.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> In comparison with other <a href="/wiki/Developed_countries" class="mw-redirect" title="Developed countries">developed countries</a>, water quality in Slovenia is considered to be among the highest in Europe. One of the reasons is undoubtedly that most of the rivers rise on the mountainous territory of Slovenia. However, this does not mean that Slovenia has no problems with surface water and groundwater quality, especially in areas with <a href="/wiki/Intensive_farming" title="Intensive farming">intensive farming</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:P_anguinus2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/P_anguinus2.jpg/220px-P_anguinus2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f1/P_anguinus2.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="207" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Olm" title="Olm">Olm</a> can be found in the <a href="/wiki/Postojna_cave" class="mw-redirect" title="Postojna cave">Postojna cave</a> and other caves in the country.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenia signed the Rio <a href="/wiki/Convention_on_Biological_Diversity" title="Convention on Biological Diversity">Convention on Biological Diversity</a> on 13 June 1992 and became a party to the convention on 9 July 1996.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> It subsequently produced a <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_action_plan" title="Biodiversity action plan">National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan</a>, which was received by the convention on 30 May 2002.
</p><p>Slovenia is distinguished by an exceptionally wide variety of habitats,<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> due to the contact of geological units and biogeographical regions, and due to human influences. The country is home to four terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests" title="Dinaric Mountains mixed forests">Dinaric Mountains mixed forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_mixed_forests" title="Pannonian mixed forests">Pannonian mixed forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alps_conifer_and_mixed_forests" title="Alps conifer and mixed forests">Alps conifer and mixed forests</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Illyrian_deciduous_forests" title="Illyrian deciduous forests">Illyrian deciduous forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Around 12.5% of the territory is protected with 35.5% in the <a href="/wiki/Natura_2000" title="Natura 2000">Natura 2000</a> ecological network.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Despite this, because of pollution and environmental degradation, diversity has been in decline. Slovenia had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 3.78/10, ranking it 140th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Animals">Animals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Animals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity" title="Biodiversity">biological diversity</a> of the country is high, with 1% of the world's organisms on 0.004% of the Earth's surface area.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> There are 75 mammal species, among them <a href="/wiki/Marmot" title="Marmot">marmots</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alpine_ibex" title="Alpine ibex">Alpine ibex</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Chamois" title="Chamois">chamois</a>. There are numerous <a href="/wiki/Deer" title="Deer">deer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roe_deer" title="Roe deer">roe deer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boar" class="mw-redirect" title="Boar">boar</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hare" title="Hare">hares</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-NFPRes_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NFPRes-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Edible_dormouse" class="mw-redirect" title="Edible dormouse">edible dormouse</a> is often found in the Slovenian beech forests. Trapping these animals is a long tradition and is a part of the Slovenian national identity.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some important carnivores include the <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_lynx" title="Eurasian lynx">Eurasian lynx</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> European <a href="/wiki/Wild_cat" class="mw-redirect" title="Wild cat">wild cats</a>, foxes (especially the <a href="/wiki/Red_fox" title="Red fox">red fox</a>), and <a href="/wiki/European_jackal" title="European jackal">European jackal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> There are <a href="/wiki/Hedgehog" title="Hedgehog">hedgehogs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marten" title="Marten">martens</a>, and snakes such as <a href="/wiki/Viperinae" title="Viperinae">vipers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Grass_snake" title="Grass snake">grass snakes</a>. According to recent estimates, Slovenia has c.&#160;40–60 <a href="/wiki/Gray_wolf" class="mw-redirect" title="Gray wolf">wolves</a><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> and about 450 <a href="/wiki/Brown_bear" title="Brown bear">brown bears</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia is home to an exceptionally diverse number of cave species, with a few tens of <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic species</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Among the cave vertebrates, the only known one is the <a href="/wiki/Olm" title="Olm">olm</a>, living in Karst, Lower Carniola, and White Carniola.
</p><p>The only regular species of <a href="/wiki/Cetacea" title="Cetacea">cetaceans</a> found in the northern Adriatic sea is the <a href="/wiki/Bottlenose_dolphin" title="Bottlenose dolphin">bottlenose dolphin</a> (<i>Tursiops truncatus</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are a wide variety of birds, such as the <a href="/wiki/Tawny_owl" title="Tawny owl">tawny owl</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Long-eared_owl" title="Long-eared owl">long-eared owl</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Eagle_owl" class="mw-redirect" title="Eagle owl">eagle owl</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hawk" title="Hawk">hawks</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Short-toed_eagle" class="mw-redirect" title="Short-toed eagle">short-toed eagles</a>. Other birds of prey have been recorded, as well as a growing number of ravens, <a href="/wiki/Crow" title="Crow">crows</a> and <a href="/wiki/Magpie" title="Magpie">magpies</a> migrating into Ljubljana and Maribor where they thrive.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Other birds include <a href="/wiki/Black_woodpecker" title="Black woodpecker">black</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_green_woodpecker" title="European green woodpecker">green</a> <a href="/wiki/Woodpecker" title="Woodpecker">woodpeckers</a> and the <a href="/wiki/White_stork" title="White stork">white stork</a>, which nests mainly in <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a>.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lipica_horses_(7198987762).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg/330px-Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="330" height="162" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg/495px-Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg/660px-Lipica_horses_%287198987762%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="787" /></a><figcaption>Modern <a href="/wiki/Lipizzaner" class="mw-redirect" title="Lipizzaner">Lipizzaner</a> grazing</figcaption></figure>
<p>There are 13 domestic animals native to Slovenia,<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> of eight species (hen, pig, dog, horse, sheep, goat, honey bee, and cattle).<sup id="cite_ref-Meglič2003_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Meglič2003-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Among these are the <a href="/wiki/Karst_Shepherd" title="Karst Shepherd">Karst Shepherd</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FCI2009doc_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FCI2009doc-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Carniolan_honeybee" class="mw-redirect" title="Carniolan honeybee">Carniolan honeybee</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Lipizzan" title="Lipizzan">Lipizzan</a> horse.<sup id="cite_ref-Meglič2003_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Meglič2003-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Marble_trout" class="mw-redirect" title="Marble trout">marble trout</a> or marmorata (<i>Salmo marmoratus</i>) is an indigenous Slovenian fish.<sup id="cite_ref-Balkan_Trout_Restoration_Group_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Balkan_Trout_Restoration_Group-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Extensive breeding programmes have been introduced to repopulate the marble trout into lakes and streams invaded by non-indigenous species of <a href="/wiki/Trout" title="Trout">trout</a>. Slovenia is also home to the <a href="/wiki/Wels_catfish" title="Wels catfish">wels catfish</a>.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Fungi">Fungi</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Fungi">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>More than 2,400 fungal species have been recorded from Slovenia<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> and, since that figure does not include lichen-forming fungi, the total number of Slovenian fungi already known is undoubtedly much higher. Many more remain to be discovered.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Plants">Plants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Plants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Slovenia is the third most-forested country in Europe,<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> with 58.3% of the territory covered by forests.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The forests are an important natural resource, and logging is kept to a minimum.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> In the interior of the country are typical Central European forests, predominantly <a href="/wiki/Oak" title="Oak">oak</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beech" title="Beech">beech</a>. In the mountains, <a href="/wiki/Spruce" title="Spruce">spruce</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fir" title="Fir">fir</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pine" title="Pine">pine</a> are more common. Pine trees grow on the <a href="/wiki/Karst_Plateau" title="Karst Plateau">Karst Plateau</a>, although only one-third of the region is covered by pine forest. The <a href="/wiki/Tilia" title="Tilia">lime/linden</a> tree, common in Slovenian forests, is a national symbol. The <a href="/wiki/Tree_line" title="Tree line">tree line</a> is at 1,700 to 1,800 metres (5,600 to 5,900 feet).<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the Alps, flowers such as <i><a href="/wiki/Daphne_blagayana" title="Daphne blagayana">Daphne blagayana</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Gentian" class="mw-redirect" title="Gentian">gentians</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Gentiana_clusii" title="Gentiana clusii">Gentiana clusii</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Gentiana_froelichii" title="Gentiana froelichii">Gentiana froelichii</a></i>), <i><a href="/wiki/Primula_auricula" title="Primula auricula">Primula auricula</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Leontopodium_alpinum" class="mw-redirect" title="Leontopodium alpinum">edelweiss</a> (the symbol of Slovene mountaineering), <i><a href="/wiki/Cypripedium_calceolus" title="Cypripedium calceolus">Cypripedium calceolus</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Fritillaria_meleagris" title="Fritillaria meleagris">Fritillaria meleagris</a></i> (snake's head fritillary), and <i><a href="/wiki/Pulsatilla_grandis" title="Pulsatilla grandis">Pulsatilla grandis</a></i> are found.
</p><p>Slovenia harbors many plants of <a href="/wiki/Ethnobotany" title="Ethnobotany">ethnobotanically</a> useful groups. Of 59 known species of ethnobotanical importance, some species such as <i><a href="/wiki/Aconitum_napellus" title="Aconitum napellus">Aconitum napellus</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Cannabis_sativa" title="Cannabis sativa">Cannabis sativa</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Taxus_baccata" title="Taxus baccata">Taxus baccata</a></i> are restricted for use as per the Official Gazette of the Republic of Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Slovenia" title="Politics of Slovenia">Politics of Slovenia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Slovenia" title="Foreign relations of Slovenia">Foreign relations of Slovenia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Robert Golob" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg/122px-Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="122" height="185" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg/183px-Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg/244px-Robert_Golob_-_52114942369_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2729" data-file-height="4137" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Robert_Golob" title="Robert Golob">Robert Golob</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Slovenia" title="Prime Minister of Slovenia">Prime Minister</a></small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Slovenia is a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary democracy</a> republic with a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> is the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Slovenia" title="President of Slovenia">president</a>, who is elected by popular vote and has an important integrative role.<sup id="cite_ref-Furtlehner_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Furtlehner-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The president is elected for five years and at maximum for two consecutive terms. The president has a representative role and is the commander-in-chief of the <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Slovenia">Slovenian armed forces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The executive and <a href="/wiki/Administration_(government)" title="Administration (government)">administrative</a> authority in Slovenia is held by the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Slovenia" title="Government of Slovenia">Government of Slovenia</a> (<i><span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">Vlada Republike Slovenije</i></span></i>),<sup id="cite_ref-Borak2004_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Borak2004-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> headed by the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Slovenia" title="Prime Minister of Slovenia">Prime Minister</a> and the council of ministers or cabinet, who are elected by the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Slovenia)" title="National Assembly (Slovenia)">National Assembly</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">Državni zbor Republike Slovenije</i></span>). The legislative authority is held by the <a href="/wiki/Bicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Bicameral">bicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of Slovenia">Parliament of Slovenia</a>, characterised by an asymmetric duality.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (April 2014)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-Prunk2007_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prunk2007-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The bulk of power is concentrated in the National Assembly, which consists of ninety members. Of those, 88 are elected by all the citizens in a system of <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a>, whereas two are elected by the registered members of the <a href="/wiki/Indigenism" title="Indigenism">autochthonous</a> Hungarian and Italian <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Minorities" class="mw-redirect" title="Ethnic Minorities">minorities</a>. Election takes place every four years. The <a href="/wiki/National_Council_(Slovenia)" title="National Council (Slovenia)">National Council</a> (<i><span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">Državni svet Republike Slovenije</i></span></i>), consisting of forty members, appointed to represent social, economic, professional and local interest groups, has a limited advisory and control power.<sup id="cite_ref-Prunk2007_130-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prunk2007-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
The 1992–2004 period was marked by the rule of the <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Democracy_of_Slovenia" title="Liberal Democracy of Slovenia">Liberal Democracy of Slovenia</a>, which was responsible for gradual transition from the <a href="/wiki/Titoism" title="Titoism">Titoist</a> economy to the capitalist market economy. It later attracted much criticism by neo-liberal economists, who demanded a less gradual approach. The party's president <a href="/wiki/Janez_Drnov%C5%A1ek" title="Janez Drnovšek">Janez Drnovšek</a>, who served as prime minister between 1992 and 2002, was one of the most influential Slovenian politicians of the 1990s,<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> alongside President <a href="/wiki/Milan_Ku%C4%8Dan" title="Milan Kučan">Milan Kučan</a> (who served between 1990 and 2002).<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2005–2008 period was characterized by over-enthusiasm after joining the EU. During the first term of <a href="/wiki/Janez_Jan%C5%A1a" title="Janez Janša">Janez Janša</a>'s government, for the first time after independence, the Slovenian banks saw their <a href="/wiki/Loan-deposit_ratio" title="Loan-deposit ratio">loan-deposit ratios</a> veering out of control. There was over-borrowing from foreign banks and then over-crediting of customers, including local <a href="/wiki/Business_magnate" title="Business magnate">business magnates</a>. After the onset of the <a href="/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%932010" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial crisis of 2007–2010">financial crisis of 2007–2010</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_sovereign-debt_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="European sovereign-debt crisis">European sovereign-debt crisis</a>, the left-wing coalition that replaced Janša's government in the 2008 elections, had to face the consequences of the 2005–2008 over-borrowing. Attempts to implement reforms that would help economic recovery were met by student protesters, led by a student who later became a member of <a href="/wiki/Janez_Jan%C5%A1a" title="Janez Janša">Janez Janša</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Slovenska_demokratska_stranka" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenska demokratska stranka">SDS</a>, and by the trade unions. The proposed reforms were postponed in a referendum. The left-wing government was ousted with a vote of no confidence.
</p><p>In March 2020, Janez Janša became prime minister for third time in the new coalition government of SDS, the <a href="/wiki/Modern_Centre_Party" title="Modern Centre Party">Modern Centre Party</a> (SMC), <a href="/wiki/New_Slovenia" title="New Slovenia">New Slovenia</a> (NSi) and Pensioners' Party (DeSUS).<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Janez Janša was known as a right-wing populist and a supporter of former US President <a href="/wiki/Donald_Trump" title="Donald Trump">Donald Trump</a> and right-wing Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n" title="Viktor Orbán">Viktor Orban</a> of Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> In April 2022, liberal opposition, the <a href="/wiki/Freedom_Movement_(Slovenia)" title="Freedom Movement (Slovenia)">Freedom Movement</a>, won the parliamentary <a href="/wiki/2022_Slovenian_parliamentary_election" title="2022 Slovenian parliamentary election">election</a>. The Freedom Movement won 34.5% of the vote, compared with 23.6% for Janša's <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Democratic_Party" title="Slovenian Democratic Party">Slovenian Democratic party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> On 25 May 2022, Slovenia's parliament voted to appoint the leader of Freedom Movement, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Golob" title="Robert Golob">Robert Golob</a>, as the new <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Slovenia" title="Prime Minister of Slovenia">Prime Minister of Slovenia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Judiciary">Judiciary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Judiciary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Slovenia" title="Judiciary of Slovenia">Judiciary of Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Judicial powers in Slovenia are executed by judges, who are elected by the National Assembly. Judicial power in Slovenia is implemented by courts with general responsibilities and specialised courts that deal with matters relating to specific legal areas. The <a href="/wiki/State%27s_Attorney" class="mw-redirect" title="State&#39;s Attorney">State Prosecutor</a> is an independent state authority responsible for prosecuting cases brought against those suspected of committing criminal offences. The <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Slovenia" title="Constitutional Court of Slovenia">Constitutional Court</a>, composed of nine judges elected for nine-year terms, decides on the conformity of laws with the Constitution; all laws and regulations must also conform with the general principles of international law and with ratified international agreements.<sup id="cite_ref-culture.si_46-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-culture.si-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Slovenia">Military of Slovenia</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Armed_Forces" title="Slovenian Armed Forces">Slovenian Armed Forces</a> provide military defence independently or within an alliance, in accordance with international agreements. Since conscription was abolished in 2003, it is organized as a fully professional <a href="/wiki/Standing_army" title="Standing army">standing army</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-Chief" class="mw-redirect" title="Commander-in-Chief">Commander-in-Chief</a> is the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Slovenia" title="President of Slovenia">President of the Republic of Slovenia</a>, while operational command is in the domain of the <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_the_General_Staff_(Slovenia)" title="Chief of the General Staff (Slovenia)">Chief of the General Staff</a> of the Slovenian Armed Forces. In 2016, military spending was an estimated 0.91% of the country's GDP. Since joining <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, the Slovenian Armed Forces have taken a more active part in supporting international peace. They have participated in peace support operations and humanitarian activities. Among others, Slovenian soldiers are a part of international forces serving in <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_interventions">International interventions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: International interventions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2022 Slovenia joined a list of nations banning Russian aircraft from its <a href="/wiki/Airspace" title="Airspace">airspace</a> as a sanction against it for invading Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions_and_traditional_regions">Administrative divisions and traditional regions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions and traditional regions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Statistical_regions_of_Slovenia" title="Statistical regions of Slovenia">Statistical regions of Slovenia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Slovenia" title="Municipalities of Slovenia">Municipalities of Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Slokfor.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Slokfor.jpg/220px-Slokfor.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Slokfor.jpg/330px-Slokfor.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Slokfor.jpg/440px-Slokfor.jpg 2x" data-file-width="647" data-file-height="452" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Armed_Forces" title="Slovenian Armed Forces">(SAF), Slovenian KFOR</a> contingent in <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Republic of Kosovo</a></figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Municipalities">Municipalities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Municipalities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Officially, Slovenia is subdivided into 212 municipalities (twelve of which have the status of urban municipalities). The municipalities are the only bodies of local autonomy in Slovenia. Each municipality is headed by a mayor (<i>župan</i>), elected every four years by popular vote, and a municipal council (<i>občinski svet</i>). In the majority of municipalities, the municipal council is elected through the system of <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a>; only a few smaller municipalities use the <a href="/wiki/Plurality_voting_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Plurality voting system">plurality voting system</a>. In the urban municipalities, the municipal councils are called town (or city) councils.<sup id="cite_ref-www2.gov.si_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www2.gov.si-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Every municipality also has a Head of the Municipal Administration (<i>načelnik občinske uprave</i>), appointed by the mayor, who is responsible for the functioning of the local administration.<sup id="cite_ref-www2.gov.si_142-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www2.gov.si-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_districts">Administrative districts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Administrative districts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>There is no official intermediate unit between the municipalities and the Republic of Slovenia. The 62 administrative districts, officially called "Administrative Units" (<i>upravne enote</i>), are only subdivisions of the national government administration and are named after their respective bases of government offices. They are headed by a Manager of the Unit (<i>načelnik upravne enote</i>), appointed by the Minister of Public Administration.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Slovenia,_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_(statistical_regions)_-_colored.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg/290px-Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="215" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg/435px-Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg/580px-Slovenia%2C_administrative_divisions_-_Nmbrs_%28statistical_regions%29_-_colored.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1160" data-file-height="859" /></a><figcaption>Statistical regions: 1.&#160;Gorizia, 2.&#160;Upper Carniola, 3.&#160;Carinthia, 4.&#160;Drava, 5.&#160;Mura, 6.&#160;Central Slovenia, 7.&#160;Central Sava, 8.&#160;Savinja, 9.&#160;Coastal–Karst, 10.&#160;Inner Carniola–Karst, 11.&#160;Southeast Slovenia, 12.&#160;Lower Sava</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Traditional_regions_and_identities">Traditional regions and identities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Traditional regions and identities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Traditional regions were based on the former <a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg" title="House of Habsburg">Habsburg</a> <a href="/wiki/Crown_land" title="Crown land">crown lands</a> that included <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Carinthia" title="Duchy of Carinthia">Carinthia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Styria_(duchy)" class="mw-redirect" title="Styria (duchy)">Styria</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Littoral</a>. Stronger than with either the Carniola as a whole, or with Slovenia as the state, Slovenes historically tend to identify themselves with the traditional regions of <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a>, and even traditional (sub)regions, such as Upper, Lower and, to a lesser extent, Inner Carniola.<sup id="cite_ref-Repe_deželani_ali_državljani_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Repe_deželani_ali_državljani-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The capital city Ljubljana was historically the administrative centre of Carniola and belonged to <a href="/wiki/Inner_Carniola" title="Inner Carniola">Inner Carniola</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Mehle_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mehle-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> except for the Šentvid district, which was in <a href="/wiki/Upper_Carniola" title="Upper Carniola">Upper Carniola</a> and also where the border between German-annexed territory and the Italian <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Ljubljana" title="Province of Ljubljana">Province of Ljubljana</a> was during the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mehle_144-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mehle-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Statistical_regions">Statistical regions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Statistical regions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The 12 <i>statistical regions</i> have no administrative function and are subdivided into two macroregions for the purpose of the <a href="/wiki/Regional_policy_of_the_European_Union" title="Regional policy of the European Union">Regional policy of the European Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
These two macroregions are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Eastern_Slovenia" title="Eastern Slovenia">Eastern Slovenia</a> (<i>Vzhodna Slovenija</i> – SI01), which groups the Mura, Drava, Carinthia, Savinja, Central Sava, Lower Sava, Southeast Slovenia, and Inner Carniola–Karst statistical regions.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Western_Slovenia" title="Western Slovenia">Western Slovenia</a> (<i>Zahodna Slovenija</i> – SI02), which groups the Central Slovenia, Upper Carniola, Gorizia, and Coastal–Karst statistical regions.</li></ul>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Slovenia" title="Economy of Slovenia">Economy of Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Slovenia has a <a href="/wiki/Developed_country" title="Developed country">developed economy</a> and is the richest Slavic country by GDP per capita.<sup id="cite_ref-rich2_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rich2-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia is also among the top global economies in terms of <a href="/wiki/Human_capital" title="Human capital">human capital</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hdi_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdi-147">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> It is the most developed transition country with an old <a href="/wiki/Coal_mining" title="Coal mining">mining</a>-<a href="/wiki/Industrial_society" title="Industrial society">industrial</a> tradition, <a href="/wiki/Chemical_industry" title="Chemical industry">chemical industry</a>, and developed <a href="/wiki/Service_economy" title="Service economy">service</a> activities.
Slovenia was in the beginning of 2007 the first new member to introduce the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a> as its currency, replacing the <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_tolar" title="Slovenian tolar">tolar</a>. Since 2010, it has been member of the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development" class="mw-redirect" title="Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development">Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-VE2012-03-15_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VE2012-03-15-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> There is a big difference in prosperity between the various regions. The economically wealthiest regions are the <a href="/wiki/Central_Slovenia_Statistical_Region" title="Central Slovenia Statistical Region">Central Slovenia</a> region, which includes the capital <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">western Slovenian regions</a> (the <a href="/wiki/Gorizia_Statistical_Region" title="Gorizia Statistical Region">Gorizia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Coastal%E2%80%93Karst_Statistical_Region" title="Coastal–Karst Statistical Region">Coastal–Karst</a> Statistical Regions), while the least wealthy regions are the <a href="/wiki/Mura_Statistical_Region" title="Mura Statistical Region">Mura</a>, <a href="/wiki/Central_Sava_Statistical_Region" title="Central Sava Statistical Region">Central Sava</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Littoral%E2%80%93Inner_Carniola_Statistical_Region" title="Littoral–Inner Carniola Statistical Region">Littoral–Inner Carniola</a> Statistical Regions.<sup id="cite_ref-bslo_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bslo-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_growth">Economic growth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Economic growth">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Slovenia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>GDP per capita development in Slovenia</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2004–06, the economy grew on average by nearly 5% a year in Slovenia; in 2007, it expanded by almost 7%. The growth surge was fuelled by debt, particularly among firms, and especially in construction. The <a href="/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%932010" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial crisis of 2007–2010">financial crisis of 2007–2010</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_sovereign-debt_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="European sovereign-debt crisis">European sovereign-debt crisis</a> had a significant impact on the domestic economy.<sup id="cite_ref-Slovenia&#39;s_Economy:_Next_in_Line_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Slovenia&#39;s_Economy:_Next_in_Line-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> The construction industry was severely hit in 2010 and 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-ST2012-03-13_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ST2012-03-13-152">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2009, Slovenian <a href="/wiki/GDP_per_capita" class="mw-redirect" title="GDP per capita">GDP per capita</a> shrank by 8%, the biggest decline in the European Union after the <a href="/wiki/Baltic_countries" class="mw-redirect" title="Baltic countries">Baltic countries</a> and Finland. An increasing burden for the Slovenian economy has been its rapidly aging population.<sup id="cite_ref-CNBC2012-01-23_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNBC2012-01-23-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In August 2012, the year-on-year contraction was 0.8%; however, 0.2% growth was recorded in the first quarter (in relation to the quarter before, after data was adjusted according to season and working days).<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Year-on-year contraction has been attributed to the fall in domestic consumption and the slowdown in export growth. The decrease in domestic consumption has been attributed to the fiscal <a href="/wiki/Austerity" title="Austerity">austerity</a>, to the freeze on budget expenditure in the final months of 2011,<sup id="cite_ref-Bloomberg_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bloomberg-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> to the failure of the efforts to implement <a href="/wiki/Microeconomic_reform" title="Microeconomic reform">economic reforms</a>, to inappropriate financing, and to the decrease in exports.<sup id="cite_ref-RTV2012-02-29_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RTV2012-02-29-156">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to the effects of the crisis, it was expected that several banks had to be bailed out by EU funds in 2013; however, needed capital was able to be covered by the country's own funds. Fiscal actions and legislations aiming on the reduction of spendings as well as several privatisations supported an economic recovery as from 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> The real economic growth rate was at 2.5% in 2016 and accelerated to 5% in 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The construction sector has seen a recent increase,<sup id="cite_ref-auto_158-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> and the tourism industry is expected to have continuous rising numbers.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-More_citations_needed_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39"
