Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Slovenia" title="Special:EditPage/Slovenia">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>&#32;in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2023</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>In recent years, Slovenia has experienced moderate economic growth, with GDP growth averaging around 2% per year between 2017 and 2019. However, like many other countries, Slovenia's economy has been impacted by the COVID-19 pandemic, with a contraction of around 5% in 2020. Overall, Slovenia's economy is relatively small but open and has shown resilience in recent years.
</p><p>Slovenia's manufacturing sector is one of the largest contributors to the country's economy, accounting for around 25% of GDP. The country has a strong tradition in manufacturing, particularly in the areas of automotive and electrical engineering. Other important sectors include services, which account for around 65% of GDP, and agriculture, forestry, and fishing, which account for around 2% of GDP.
</p><p>Slovenia is a highly export-oriented economy, with exports accounting for around 80% of GDP. The country's main export partners are other European countries, particularly Germany, Italy, and Austria. Key exports include machinery and transport equipment, manufactured goods, and chemicals.
</p><p>The government of Slovenia has implemented a range of policies aimed at promoting economic growth and development. These include efforts to attract foreign investment, reduce red tape, and increase investment in research and development. The country has also introduced reforms aimed at improving the efficiency of its labor market and increasing the flexibility of its economy.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="National_debt">National debt</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: National debt">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Slovenia's total <a href="/wiki/Government_debt" title="Government debt">national debt</a> rose substantially during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Recession" title="Great Recession">Great Recession</a> and was decreasing as of 2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>; at the end of 2018 amounted to 32,223&#160;million euros, 70% of the GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Services_and_industry">Services and industry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Services and industry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Almost two-thirds of people are employed in services, and over one-third in industry and construction.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia benefits from a well-educated workforce, well-developed infrastructure, and its location at the crossroads of major trade routes.<sup id="cite_ref-VE2012-03-15_148-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VE2012-03-15-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The level of <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a>&#160;(FDI) per capita in Slovenia is one of the lowest in the EU,<sup id="cite_ref-VE2012-03-15_148-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VE2012-03-15-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> and the labor productivity and the competitiveness of the Slovenian economy is still significantly below the EU average.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Taxes are relatively high, the <a href="/wiki/Labor_market" class="mw-redirect" title="Labor market">labor market</a> is seen by business interests as being inflexible, and industries are losing sales to China, India, and elsewhere.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>High level of openness makes Slovenia extremely sensitive to economic conditions in its main trading partners and changes in its international price competitiveness.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> The main industries are motor vehicles, electric and electronic equipment, machinery, <a href="/wiki/Pharmaceutical" class="mw-redirect" title="Pharmaceutical">pharmaceuticals</a>, and fuels.<sup id="cite_ref-VE2012-03-15_148-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VE2012-03-15-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Examples of major Slovenian companies operating in Slovenia include the home appliance manufacturer <a href="/wiki/Gorenje" title="Gorenje">Gorenje</a>, the pharmaceutical companies <a href="/wiki/Krka_(company)" title="Krka (company)">Krka</a> and Lek (<a href="/wiki/Novartis" title="Novartis">Novartis</a>' subsidiary), the oil distributing company <a href="/wiki/Petrol_Group" title="Petrol Group">Petrol Group</a>, energy distribution companys GEN, GEN-I, HSE and <a href="/wiki/Revoz" title="Revoz">Revoz</a>, a manufacturing subsidiary of <a href="/wiki/Renault" title="Renault">Renault</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Energy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Slovenia" title="Energy in Slovenia">Energy in Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg/220px-Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg/330px-Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg/440px-Nuklearna_elektrarna_Kr%C5%A1ko.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4080" data-file-height="2720" /></a><figcaption>Krško Nuclear Power Plant, 696 MW</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2018, the net energy production was 12,262&#160;GWh and consumption was 14,501&#160;GWh. Hydroelectric plants produced 4,421&#160;GWh, thermal plants produced 4,049&#160;GWh, and the <a href="/wiki/Kr%C5%A1ko_Nuclear_Power_Plant" title="Krško Nuclear Power Plant">Krško Nuclear Power Plant</a> produced 2,742&#160;GWh (50% share that goes to Slovenia; other 50% goes to Croatia due to joint ownership). 
Domestic electricity consumption was covered 84.6% by domestic production; percentage is decreasing from year to year meaning Slovenia is more and more depending on electricity import.<sup id="cite_ref-agencija_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-agencija-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A new 600&#160;MW block of <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0o%C5%A1tanj#Power_plant" title="Šoštanj">Šoštanj thermal power plant</a> finished construction and went online in the autumn of 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> The new 39.5&#160;MW HE Krško hydro power plant was finished in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> The 41.5&#160;MW HE Brežice and 30.5&#160;MW HE Mokrice hydro power plants were built on the <a href="/wiki/Sava" title="Sava">Sava</a> River in 2018 and the construction of ten more hydropower plants with a cumulative capacity of 338&#160;MW is planned to be finished by 2030. A large <a href="/wiki/Pumped-storage_hydroelectricity" title="Pumped-storage hydroelectricity">pumped-storage hydro power plant</a> Kozjak on the <a href="/wiki/Drava" title="Drava">Drava</a> River is in the planning stage.
</p><p>At the end of 2018, at least 295&#160;MWp of <a href="/wiki/Photovoltaics" title="Photovoltaics">photovoltaic modules</a> and 31,4&#160;MW of <a href="/wiki/Biogas_power_plant" class="mw-redirect" title="Biogas power plant">biogas powerplants</a> were installed. Compared to 2017, renewable energy sources contributed 5.6 percentage points more into whole energy consumption. There is interest to add more production in the area of solar and wind energy sources (subsidising schemes are increasing economic feasibility), but microlocation settlement procedures take enormous toll on the efficiency of this intitiatve (nature preservation vs. energy production facilities dilemma).<sup id="cite_ref-agencija_169-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-agencija-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Postojna Cave" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg/220px-Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg/330px-Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg/440px-Postojna_Cave._2012-07-26_13-41-13.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Postojna_Cave" title="Postojna Cave">Postojna Cave</a></figcaption></figure><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Slovenia" title="Tourism in Slovenia">Tourism in Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Slovenia offers tourists a wide variety of natural and cultural amenities. Different forms of tourism have developed. The tourist gravitational area is considerably large, however the tourist market is small. There has been no large-scale tourism and no acute environmental pressures;<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> in 2017, <a href="/wiki/National_Geographic" title="National Geographic">National Geographic</a> Traveller's Magazine declared Slovenia as the country with the world's most <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_tourism" title="Sustainable tourism">sustainable tourism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The nation's capital, Ljubljana, has many important <a href="/wiki/Baroque" title="Baroque">Baroque</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vienna_Secession" title="Vienna Secession">Vienna Secession</a> buildings, with several important works of the native born architect <a href="/wiki/Jo%C5%BEe_Ple%C4%8Dnik" title="Jože Plečnik">Jože Plečnik</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the northwestern corner of the country lie the <a href="/wiki/Julian_Alps" title="Julian Alps">Julian Alps</a> with <a href="/wiki/Lake_Bled" title="Lake Bled">Lake Bled</a> and the <a href="/wiki/So%C4%8Da" title="Soča">Soča</a> Valley, as well as the nation's highest peak, <a href="/wiki/Mount_Triglav" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Triglav">Mount Triglav</a> in the middle of <a href="/wiki/Triglav_National_Park" title="Triglav National Park">Triglav National Park</a>. Other mountain ranges include <a href="/wiki/Kamnik%E2%80%93Savinja_Alps" title="Kamnik–Savinja Alps">Kamnik–Savinja Alps</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Karawanks" title="Karawanks">Karawanks</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pohorje" title="Pohorje">Pohorje</a>, popular with skiers and hikers.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Karst_Plateau" title="Karst Plateau">Karst Plateau</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a> gave its name to <a href="/wiki/Karst" title="Karst">karst</a>, a landscape shaped by water dissolving the carbonate bedrock, forming caves. The best-known caves are <a href="/wiki/Postojna_Cave" title="Postojna Cave">Postojna Cave</a> and the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO</a>-listed <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0kocjan_Caves" title="Škocjan Caves">Škocjan Caves</a>. The region of <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Istria" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian Istria">Slovenian Istria</a> meets the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a>, where the most important historical monument is the <a href="/wiki/Venetian_Gothic_architecture" title="Venetian Gothic architecture">Venetian Gothic</a> Mediterranean town of <a href="/wiki/Piran" title="Piran">Piran</a> while the settlement of <a href="/wiki/Portoro%C5%BE" title="Portorož">Portorož</a> attracts crowds in summer.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:View_of_Bled_Island_(6).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Lake Bled" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg/220px-View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg/330px-View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg/440px-View_of_Bled_Island_%286%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3974" data-file-height="2649" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Lake_Bled" title="Lake Bled">Lake Bled</a> with its island</figcaption></figure><p>The hills around Slovenia's second-largest city, <a href="/wiki/Maribor" title="Maribor">Maribor</a>, are renowned for their wine-making. The northeastern part of the country is rich with spas,<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> with <a href="/wiki/Roga%C5%A1ka_Slatina" title="Rogaška Slatina">Rogaška Slatina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Radenci" title="Radenci">Radenci</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C4%8Cate%C5%BE_ob_Savi" title="Čatež ob Savi">Čatež ob Savi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dobrna" title="Dobrna">Dobrna</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Moravske_Toplice" title="Moravske Toplice">Moravske Toplice</a> growing in importance in the last two decades.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other popular tourist destinations include the historic cities of <a href="/wiki/Ptuj" title="Ptuj">Ptuj</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0kofja_Loka" title="Škofja Loka">Škofja Loka</a>, and several castles, such as <a href="/wiki/Predjama_Castle" title="Predjama Castle">Predjama Castle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Important parts of tourism in Slovenia include congress and <a href="/wiki/Gambling_tourism" class="mw-redirect" title="Gambling tourism">gambling tourism</a>. Slovenia is the country with the highest percentage of <a href="/wiki/Casino" title="Casino">casinos</a> per 1,000&#160;inhabitants in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Perla_hotel_and_casino&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Perla hotel and casino (page does not exist)">Perla</a> in <a href="/wiki/Nova_Gorica" title="Nova Gorica">Nova Gorica</a> is the largest casino in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most of foreign tourists to Slovenia come from the key European markets: Italy, <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a>, Germany, <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Benelux" title="Benelux">Benelux</a>, <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>, Russia and <a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukraine</a>, followed by UK and Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> European tourists create more than 90% of Slovenia's tourist income. In 2016, Slovenia was declared the world's first green country by the Netherlands-based organization Green Destinations.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> On being declared the most sustainable country in 2016, Slovenia had a big part to play at the <a href="/wiki/ITB_Berlin" title="ITB Berlin">ITB Berlin</a> to promote sustainable tourism.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Slovenia" title="Transport in Slovenia">Transport in Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Geography has dictated transport routes in Slovenia. Significant mountain ranges, major rivers and proximity to the Danube played roles in the development of the area's transportation corridors. One recent particular advantage are the <a href="/wiki/Pan-European_transport_corridor" class="mw-redirect" title="Pan-European transport corridor">Pan-European transport corridors</a> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pan-European_transport_corridor_V&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pan-European transport corridor V (page does not exist)">V</a> (the fastest link between the North Adriatic, and Central and Eastern Europe) and <a href="/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_X" title="Pan-European Corridor X">X</a> (linking Central Europe with the Balkans). This gives it a special position in the European social, economic and cultural integration and restructuring.<sup id="cite_ref-UKOM2000-11_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UKOM2000-11-185">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg/300px-Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="196" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg/450px-Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg/600px-Motorways-SLO-map_2018.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1840" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>Motorways in Slovenia in August 2020</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Roads">Roads</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Roads">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The road freight and passenger transport constitutes the largest part of transport in Slovenia at 80%.<sup id="cite_ref-MW2009SORS_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MW2009SORS-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Personal cars are much more popular than public road passenger transport, which has significantly declined.<sup id="cite_ref-MW2009SORS_186-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MW2009SORS-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia has a very high <a href="/wiki/Highways_in_Slovenia" title="Highways in Slovenia">highway and motorway density</a> compared to the European Union average.<sup id="cite_ref-ECORYS2011_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECORYS2011-188">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> The highway system, the construction of which was accelerated after 1994,<sup id="cite_ref-Oplotnik2004_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Oplotnik2004-189">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> has slowly but steadily transformed Slovenia into a large <a href="/wiki/Conurbation" title="Conurbation">conurbation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Other state roads have been rapidly deteriorating because of neglect and the overall increase in traffic.<sup id="cite_ref-ECORYS2011_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECORYS2011-188">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Railways">Railways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Railways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Railways" title="Slovenian Railways">Slovenian Railways</a></div>
<p>The existing Slovenian railways are out-of-date and have difficulty competing with the motorway network; partially also as a result of dispersed population settlement.<sup id="cite_ref-ARSO420_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARSO420-191">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> Due to this fact and the projected increase in traffic through the port of <a href="/wiki/Koper" title="Koper">Koper</a>, which is primarily by train, a second rail on the Koper-Divača route is in early stages of starting construction.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> With a lack of financial assets, maintenance and modernisation of the Slovenian railway network have been neglected.<sup id="cite_ref-UNIMB2011-04_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNIMB2011-04-193">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> Due to the out-of-date infrastructure, the share of the railway freight transport has been in decline in Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-ARS416_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARS416-194">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> The railway passenger transport has been recovering after a large drop in the 1990s.<sup id="cite_ref-ARS416_194-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARS416-194">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> The Pan-European railway corridors V and X, and several other major European rail lines intersect in Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-UNIMB2011-04_193-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNIMB2011-04-193">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ports">Ports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Ports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The major Slovenian port is the <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Koper" title="Port of Koper">Port of Koper</a>. It is the largest Northern Adriatic port in terms of container transport,<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> with almost 590,000 <a href="/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit" title="Twenty-foot equivalent unit">TEUs</a> annually<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> and lines to all major world ports.<sup id="cite_ref-ECONSHIP2011_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECONSHIP2011-197">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AH2008-16-3GA_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AH2008-16-3GA-198">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> It is much closer to destinations east of the <a href="/wiki/Suez_Canal" title="Suez Canal">Suez</a> than the ports of Northern Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-ECONSHIP2011_197-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECONSHIP2011-197">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the maritime passenger traffic mostly takes place in Koper.<sup id="cite_ref-RR21_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR21-199">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> Two smaller ports used for the international passenger transport as well as cargo transport are located in <a href="/wiki/Izola" title="Izola">Izola</a> and <a href="/wiki/Piran" title="Piran">Piran</a>. Passenger transport mainly takes place with Italy and Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-ReNPRP_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReNPRP-200">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Splo%C5%A1na_plovba&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Splošna plovba (page does not exist)">Splošna plovba</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> the only Slovenian shipping company, transports freight and is active only in foreign ports.<sup id="cite_ref-RR21_199-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR21-199">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Air">Air</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Air">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Air transport in Slovenia is quite low,<sup id="cite_ref-ARS416_194-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARS416-194">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> but has significantly grown since 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-RR26_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR26-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Of the three international airports in Slovenia, <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Jo%C5%BEe_Pu%C4%8Dnik_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Ljubljana Jože Pučnik Airport">Ljubljana Jože Pučnik Airport</a> in central Slovenia is the busiest,<sup id="cite_ref-RR26_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR26-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> with connections to many major European destinations.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Maribor_Edvard_Rusjan_Airport" title="Maribor Edvard Rusjan Airport">Maribor Edvard Rusjan Airport</a> is located in the eastern part of the country and the <a href="/wiki/Portoro%C5%BE_Airport" title="Portorož Airport">Portorož Airport</a> in the west.<sup id="cite_ref-RR26_202-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR26-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The state-owned <a href="/wiki/Adria_Airways" title="Adria Airways">Adria Airways</a> is the largest Slovenian airline; however in 2019 it declared bankruptcy and ceased operations.<sup id="cite_ref-RR26_202-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR26-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Since 2003, several new carriers have entered the market, mainly low-cost airlines.<sup id="cite_ref-ECORYS2011_188-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECORYS2011-188">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> The only Slovenian <a href="/wiki/Military_airbase" class="mw-redirect" title="Military airbase">military airport</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Cerklje_ob_Krki_Air_Base" class="mw-redirect" title="Cerklje ob Krki Air Base">Cerklje ob Krki Air Base</a> in the southwest.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> There are also 12&#160;<a href="/wiki/Public_airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Public airport">public airports</a> in Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-RR26_202-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RR26-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Slovenia" title="Demographics of Slovenia">Demographics of Slovenia</a></div>
<table class="toccolours" style="width:15em;border-top-width:0;border-spacing: 0;float:right;clear:right;margin:0.5em 0 1em 0.5em;"><caption style="border-top:1px #aaa solid;border-left:1px #aaa <figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Population_density_in_Slovenia.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Population_density_in_Slovenia.png/300px-Population_density_in_Slovenia.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="183" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Population_density_in_Slovenia.png/450px-Population_density_in_Slovenia.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Population_density_in_Slovenia.png/600px-Population_density_in_Slovenia.png 2x" data-file-width="870" data-file-height="530" /></a><figcaption>Population density in Slovenia by <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Slovenia" title="Municipalities of Slovenia">municipality</a>. The four main urban areas are visible: Ljubljana and Kranj (centre), Maribor (northeast) and the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Istria" title="Slovene Istria">Slovene Istria</a> (southwest).</figcaption></figure>
<p>With 101 inhabitants per square kilometer (262/sq&#160;mi), Slovenia ranks low among the European countries in population density (compared to 402/km<sup>2</sup> (1,040/sq&#160;mi) for the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Netherlands</a> or 195/km<sup>2</sup> (510/sq&#160;mi) for Italy). The <a href="/wiki/Inner_Carniola%E2%80%93Karst_Statistical_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Inner Carniola–Karst Statistical Region">Inner Carniola–Karst Statistical Region</a> has the lowest population density while the <a href="/wiki/Central_Slovenia_Statistical_Region" title="Central Slovenia Statistical Region">Central Slovenia Statistical Region</a> has the highest.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia is among the European countries with the most pronounced ageing of its population, ascribable to a low birth rate and increasing life expectancy.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Almost all Slovenian inhabitants older than 64 are retired, with no significant difference between the genders.<sup id="cite_ref-Hoff2011_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hoff2011-207">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> The working-age group is diminishing in spite of immigration.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The proposal to raise the retirement age from the current 57 for women and 58 for men was rejected in a <a href="/wiki/June_2011_Slovenian_referendum" title="June 2011 Slovenian referendum">referendum in 2011</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CNBC2012-01-23_153-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNBC2012-01-23-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the difference among the genders regarding life expectancy is still significant.<sup id="cite_ref-Hoff2011_207-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hoff2011-207">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (TFR) in 2014 was estimated at 1.33 children born/woman, which is lower than the replacement rate of 2.1.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> The majority of children are born to unmarried women (in 2016, 58.6% of all births were outside of marriage).<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
In 2018, life expectancy at birth was 81.1 years (78.2 years male, and 84 years female).<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2009, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_suicide_rate" title="List of countries by suicide rate">suicide rate</a> in Slovenia was 22 per 100,000 persons per year, which places Slovenia among the highest ranked European countries.<sup id="cite_ref-stat2009suicide_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat2009suicide-212">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, from 2000 until 2010, the rate has decreased by about 30%. The differences between regions and the genders are pronounced.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Urbanisation">Urbanisation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Urbanisation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Slovenia" title="List of cities and towns in Slovenia">List of cities and towns in Slovenia</a></div>
<p>Depending on definition, between 65% and 79% of people live in wider urban areas.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a> definition of rural areas none of the <a href="/wiki/Statistical_regions_of_Slovenia" title="Statistical regions of Slovenia">Slovene statistical regions</a> is mostly urbanised, meaning that 15% or less of the population lives in rural communities. According to this definition statistical regions are classified:
</p>
<ul><li>mostly rural regions: <a href="/wiki/Mura_Statistical_Region" title="Mura Statistical Region">Mura</a>, <a href="/wiki/Drava_Statistical_Region" title="Drava Statistical Region">Drava</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carinthia_Statistical_Region" title="Carinthia Statistical Region">Carinthia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Savinja_Statistical_Region" title="Savinja Statistical Region">Savinja</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lower_Sava_Statistical_Region" title="Lower Sava Statistical Region">Lower Sava</a>, <a href="/wiki/Littoral%E2%80%93Inner_Carniola_Statistical_Region" title="Littoral–Inner Carniola Statistical Region">Littoral–Inner Carniola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gorizia_Statistical_Region" title="Gorizia Statistical Region">Gorizia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Slovenia_Statistical_Region" title="Southeast Slovenia Statistical Region">Southeast Slovenia</a></li>
<li>moderately rural regions: <a href="/wiki/Central_Sava_Statistical_Region" title="Central Sava Statistical Region">Central Sava</a>, <a href="/wiki/Upper_Carniola_Statistical_Region" title="Upper Carniola Statistical Region">Upper Carniola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coastal%E2%80%93Karst_Statistical_Region" title="Coastal–Karst Statistical Region">Coastal–Karst</a>, <a href="/wiki/Central_Slovenia_Statistical_Region" title="Central Slovenia Statistical Region">Central Slovenia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>The only large town is the capital, Ljubljana. Other (medium-sized) towns include Maribor, Celje, and Kranj.<sup id="cite_ref-GV52-2_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GV52-2-216">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Overall, there are eleven urban municipalities in Slovenia.
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1148739982">.mw-parser-output .largestCities-table-background{background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .largestCities-cell-background{background:#f0f0f0}</style>
</p>
<div>

<td rowspan="11" style="text-align: center"><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Ljubljana"><img alt="Ljubljana" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg/120px-Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg" decoding="async" width="120" height="80" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg/180px-Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg/240px-Ljubljana_made_by_Janez_Kotar.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="853" /></a></span><br /><a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a><br /><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Maribor"><img alt="Maribor" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg/120px-19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg" decoding="async" width="120" height="90" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg/180px-19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg/240px-19-03-03-Maribor-RalfR-DJI_0444.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4048" data-file-height="3032" /></a></span><br /><a href="/wiki/Maribor" title="Maribor">Maribor</a>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Slovenia" title="Languages of Slovenia">Languages of Slovenia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene language</a></div>
<p>The official language in Slovenia is <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a>, which is a member of the <a href="/wiki/South_Slavic_languages" title="South Slavic languages">South Slavic language group</a>. In 2002, Slovene was the native language of around 88% of Slovenia's population according to the census, with more than 92% of the Slovenian population speaking it in their home environment.<sup id="cite_ref-siol.net_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-siol.net-218">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-stat.si_219-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.si-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> This statistic ranks Slovenia among the most homogeneous countries in the EU in terms of the share of speakers of the predominant mother tongue.<sup id="cite_ref-Dular_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dular-220">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovene is a highly diverse Slavic language in terms of <a href="/wiki/Slovene_dialects" title="Slovene dialects">dialects</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-MLD_2009_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MLD_2009-221">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> with different degrees of mutual intelligibility. Accounts of the number of dialects range from as few as seven<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> dialects, often considered dialect groups or dialect bases that are further subdivided into as many as 50 dialects.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Other sources characterize the number of dialects as nine<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> or as eight.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Slovenian_Passport3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Slovenian_Passport3.jpg/170px-Slovenian_Passport3.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="241" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Slovenian_Passport3.jpg/255px-Slovenian_Passport3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Slovenian_Passport3.jpg/340px-Slovenian_Passport3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="635" data-file-height="901" /></a><figcaption>Front cover of a bilingual passport in Slovene and Italian</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Italian_language_in_Slovenia" title="Italian language in Slovenia">Italian</a>, spoken by the respective minorities, enjoy the status of official languages in the ethnically mixed regions along the Hungarian and Italian borders, to the extent that even the passports issued in those areas are bilingual. In 2002 around 0.2% of the Slovenian population spoke Italian and around 0.4% spoke Hungarian as their native language. Hungarian is co-official with Slovene in 30 settlements in 5 municipalities (whereof 3 are officially bilingual). Italian is co-official with Slovene in 25 settlements in 4 municipalities (all of them officially bilingual).
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Romani_language" title="Romani language">Romani</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> spoken in 2002 as the native language by 0.2% of people, is a legally protected language in Slovenia. Romani speakers mainly belong to the geographically dispersed and marginalized Roma community.<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>German, which used to be the largest minority language in Slovenia prior to World War II (around 4% of the population in 1921), is now the native language of only around 0.08% of the population, the majority of whom are more than 60 years old.<sup id="cite_ref-stat.si_219-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.si-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Gottscheerish" title="Gottscheerish">Gottscheerish</a> or <i>Granish</i>, the traditional German dialect of <a href="/wiki/Gottschee" title="Gottschee">Gottschee</a> County, faces extinction.<sup id="cite_ref-norwaysi_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-norwaysi-230">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A significant number of people in Slovenia speak a variant of <a href="/wiki/Serbo-Croatian" title="Serbo-Croatian">Serbo-Croatian</a> (<a href="/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language">Serbian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_language" title="Bosnian language">Bosnian</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>) as their native language. These are mostly families who moved to Slovenia from other <a href="/wiki/Former_Yugoslav" class="mw-redirect" title="Former Yugoslav">former Yugoslav</a> republics. Altogether, Serbo-Croatian in its different forms is the second natively spoken language in Slovenia with 5.9% of population. In 2002, 0.4% of the Slovenian population declared themselves to be native speakers of <a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a> and 0.2% native speakers of <a href="/wiki/Macedonian_language" title="Macedonian language">Macedonian</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-stat.si_219-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.si-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Czech_language" title="Czech language">Czech</a>, the fourth-largest minority language in Slovenia prior to World War II (after German, Hungarian, and Serbo-Croatian), is now the native language of a few hundred residents of Slovenia.<sup id="cite_ref-stat.si_219-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.si-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia ranks among the top European countries in knowledge of foreign languages. The most taught foreign languages are English, German, Italian, French and Spanish. As of 2007<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 92% of the population between the age of 25 and 64 spoke at least one foreign language and around 71.8% of them spoke at least two foreign languages, which was the highest percentage in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> According to the <a href="/wiki/Eurobarometer" title="Eurobarometer">Eurobarometer</a> survey, as of 2005<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> the majority of Slovenes could speak <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a> (61%) and English (56%).<sup id="cite_ref-EurobarLang_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EurobarLang-232">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 21">&#58;&#8202;21&#8202;</span></sup> A reported 42% of Slovenes could speak German, which was one of the highest percentages outside German-speaking countries.<sup id="cite_ref-EurobarLang_232-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EurobarLang-232">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> Italian is widely spoken on the <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Istria" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian Istria">Slovenian Coast</a> and in some other areas of the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a>. Around 15% of Slovenians can speak Italian, which is (according to the Eurobarometer pool) the third-highest percentage in the European Union, after Italy and <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Immigration">Immigration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Immigration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2015, about 12% (237,616 people) of the population in Slovenia was born abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-SURS_234-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SURS-234">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> About 86% of the foreign-born population originated from other countries of former Yugoslavia as (in descending order) <a href="/wiki/Bosnia-Herzegovina" class="mw-redirect" title="Bosnia-Herzegovina">Bosnia-Herzegovina</a>, followed by immigrants from <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>, <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SURS_234-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SURS-234">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By the beginning of 2017, there were about 114,438 people with foreign citizenship residing in the country making up 5.5% of the total population. Of these foreigners, 76% had citizenships of the other countries from former Yugoslavia (excluding Croatia). Additionally 16.4% had EU-citizenships and 7.6% had citizenships of other countries.<sup id="cite_ref-SURS_234-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SURS-234">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">

</div>
</div>
<p>According to the 2002 census, Slovenia's main ethnic group are <a href="/wiki/Slovenes" title="Slovenes">Slovenes</a> (83%); however, their share in the total population is continuously decreasing, due to their relatively low <a href="/wiki/Fertility_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Fertility rate">fertility rate</a>. At least 13% (2002) of the population were immigrants from other parts of <a href="/wiki/Former_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Former Yugoslavia">Former Yugoslavia</a> and their descendants.<sup id="cite_ref-Medvesek_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Medvesek-235">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> They have settled mainly in cities and suburbanised areas.<sup id="cite_ref-Repolusk_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Repolusk-236">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> Relatively small but protected by the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Slovenia" title="Constitution of Slovenia">Constitution of Slovenia</a> are the <a href="/wiki/Hungarians_in_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Hungarians in Slovenia">Hungarian</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Italians_in_Slovenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Italians in Slovenia">Italian ethnic minority</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-MDL2010_237-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MDL2010-237">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Constitution_238-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Constitution-238">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> A special position is held by the autochthonous and geographically dispersed <a href="/w/index.php?title=Roma_in_Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Roma in Slovenia (page does not exist)">Roma ethnic community</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The number of people immigrating into Slovenia rose steadily from 1995<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> and has been increasing even more rapidly in recent years. After Slovenia joined the EU in 2004, the annual number of immigrants doubled by 2006 and increased by half yet again by 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> In 2007, Slovenia had one of the fastest growing <a href="/wiki/Net_migration_rate" title="Net migration rate">net migration rates</a> in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_242-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Slovenia" title="Religion in Slovenia">Religion in Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg/170px-Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg/255px-Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg/340px-Radovljica_Brezje_Bazilika_Sv_Vida_28082012_122.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2832" data-file-height="4256" /></a><figcaption>The National Shrine Mary Help of Christians at <a href="/wiki/Brezje,_Radovljica" title="Brezje, Radovljica">Brezje</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Before World War II, 97% of the Slovenian population identified as members of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Slovenia" title="Catholic Church in Slovenia">Catholic Church in the country</a>, around 2.5% as Lutheran, and around 0.5% of residents identified themselves as members of other denominations.<sup id="cite_ref-stat.si_219-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.si-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> After 1945, the country underwent a process of gradual but steady <a href="/wiki/Secularization" title="Secularization">secularization</a>. After a decade of persecution of religions, the Communist regime adopted a policy of relative tolerance towards churches. After 1990, the Catholic Church regained some of its former influence, but Slovenia remains a largely secularized society. 
</p><p>The 2018 Eurobarometer data shows 73.4% of population identifying as Catholic<sup id="cite_ref-EB2018_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB2018-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> that fell to 72.1% in the 2019 Eurobarometer survey.<sup id="cite_ref-EB2019_244-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB2019-244">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> According to the Catholic Church data, the Catholic population fell from 78.04% in 2009 to 72.11% in 2019<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> The vast majority of Slovenian Catholics belong to the <a href="/wiki/Latin_Church" title="Latin Church">Latin Church</a>. A small number of <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholics</a> live in the <a href="/wiki/White_Carniola" title="White Carniola">White Carniola</a> region.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">

</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Slovenia (2019)<sup id="cite_ref-EB2019_244-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB2019-244">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:DarkBlue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Slovenia" title="Catholic Church in Slovenia">Catholic</a> (72.1%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;None (18%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:DarkOrchid; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox</a> (3.7%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:yellow; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> (0.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:SkyBlue; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other Christian (1%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslim</a> (3%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#C3B091; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other religion (3%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:red; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Undeclared (2%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>Despite a relatively small number of <a href="/wiki/Protestants" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestants">Protestants</a> (less than 1% in 2002), the Protestant legacy is historically significant given that the Slovene standard language and Slovene literature were established by the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation">Protestant Reformation</a>. <a href="/wiki/Primoz_Trubar" class="mw-redirect" title="Primoz Trubar">Primoz Trubar</a>, a theologian in the <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a> tradition, was one of the most influential <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformers" title="Protestant Reformers">Protestant Reformers</a> in Slovenia. Protestantism was extinguished in the <a href="/wiki/Counter-Reformation" title="Counter-Reformation">Counter-Reformation</a> implemented by the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Habsburg dynasty">Habsburg dynasty</a>, which controlled the region. It only survived in the easternmost regions due to protection of Hungarian nobles, who often happened to be <a href="/wiki/Calvinist" class="mw-redirect" title="Calvinist">Calvinist</a> themselves. Today, a significant <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheran</a> minority lives in the easternmost region of <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a>, where they represent around a fifth of the population and are headed by a bishop with the seat in <a href="/wiki/Murska_Sobota" title="Murska Sobota">Murska Sobota</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The third largest denomination, with around 2.2% of the population, is the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>, with most adherents belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church">Serbian Orthodox Church</a> while a minority belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Macedonian_Orthodox_Church" title="Macedonian Orthodox Church">Macedonian</a> and other Eastern Orthodox churches.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>According to the 2002 census, <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> is the second largest religious denomination in the country, with around 2.4% of the population. Most Slovenian Muslims came from <a href="/wiki/Bosnia" class="mw-redirect" title="Bosnia">Bosnia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slovenia has long been home to a <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Slovenia" title="History of the Jews in Slovenia">Jewish community</a>. Despite the losses suffered during the <a href="/wiki/Holocaust" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust">Holocaust</a>, Judaism still numbers a few hundred adherents, mostly living in Ljubljana, site of the sole remaining active synagogue in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 2002, around 10% of Slovenes declared themselves <a href="/wiki/Atheists" class="mw-redirect" title="Atheists">atheists</a>, another 10% professed no specific denomination, and around 16% declined to answer. According to the <a href="/wiki/Eurobarometer" title="Eurobarometer">Eurobarometer</a> Poll 2010,<sup id="cite_ref-:0_250-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-250">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> 32% of Slovenian citizens "believe there is a god", whereas 36% "believe there is some sort of spirit or life force" and 26% "do not believe there is any sort of spirit, god, or life force".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_250-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-250">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Slovenia" title="Education in Slovenia">Education in Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg/220px-University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg/330px-University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg/440px-University_of_Ljubljana_Palace.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4584" data-file-height="3400" /></a><figcaption>University of Ljubljana administration building</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenia's education ranks as the 12th best in the world and 4th best in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, being significantly higher than the <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a> average, according to the <a href="/wiki/Programme_for_International_Student_Assessment" title="Programme for International Student Assessment">Programme for International Student Assessment</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> Among people age 25 to 64, 12% have attended higher education, while on average Slovenes have 9.6 years of formal education. According to an OECD report, 83% of adults ages 25–64 have earned the equivalent of a high school degree, well above the OECD average of 74%; among 25- to 34-year-olds, the rate is 93%.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> According to the 1991 census there is 99.6% <a href="/wiki/Literacy" title="Literacy">literacy</a> in Slovenia. <a href="/wiki/Lifelong_learning" title="Lifelong learning">Lifelong learning</a> is also increasing.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-253">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Responsibility for education oversight at primary and secondary level in Slovenia lies with the Ministry of Education and Sports. After non-compulsory pre-school education, children enter the nine-year primary school at the age of six.<sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> Primary school is divided into three periods, each of three years. In the academic year 2006–2007 there were 166,000 pupils enrolled in elementary education and more than 13,225 teachers, giving a ratio of one teacher per 12 pupils and 20 pupils per class.
</p><p>After completing elementary school, nearly all children (more than 98%) go on to secondary education, either vocational, technical or general secondary programmes (<a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">gimnazija</a>). The latter concludes with <a href="/wiki/Matura#Matura_in_Slovenia" title="Matura">matura</a>, the final exam that allows the graduates to enter a university. 84% of secondary school graduates go on to tertiary education.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_253-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-253">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Among several universities in Slovenia, the best ranked is the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Ljubljana" title="University of Ljubljana">University of Ljubljana</a>, ranking among the first 500 or the first 3% of the world's best universities according to the <a href="/wiki/Academic_Ranking_of_World_Universities" title="Academic Ranking of World Universities">ARWU</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> Two other public universities include the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Maribor" title="University of Maribor">University of Maribor</a><sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/Styria_(Slovenia)" title="Styria (Slovenia)">Styria</a> region and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Primorska" title="University of Primorska">University of Primorska</a> in <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> In addition, there is a private <a href="/wiki/University_of_Nova_Gorica" title="University of Nova Gorica">University of Nova Gorica</a><sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> and an international <a href="/wiki/EMUNI_University" class="mw-redirect" title="EMUNI University">EMUNI University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Cleanup plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-style ambox-Cleanup" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/40px-Edit-clear.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="40" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/60px-Edit-clear.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/f2/Edit-clear.svg/80px-Edit-clear.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="48" data-file-height="48" /></span></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article may <b>require <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Cleanup" title="Wikipedia:Cleanup">cleanup</a></b> to meet Wikipedia's <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style">quality standards</a>. The specific problem is: <b>low amount of data mainly name spamming.</b><span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit">improve this article</a> if you can.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">September 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Slovenia" title="Culture of Slovenia">Culture of Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg/220px-Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="196" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg/330px-Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg/440px-Ivan_Grohar_-_Sejalec.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1427" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/The_Sower_(Grohar)" title="The Sower (Grohar)">The Sower</a></i> (1907), by the Impressionist painter <a href="/wiki/Ivan_Grohar" title="Ivan Grohar">Ivan Grohar</a>, became a <a href="/wiki/Metaphor" title="Metaphor">metaphor</a> for Slovenes<sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup> and was a reflection of the transition from a rural to an urban culture.<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Heritage">Heritage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Heritage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Slovenia's architectural heritage includes 2,500 churches, 1,000 castles, ruins, and manor houses, farmhouses, and special structures for drying hay, called <a href="/wiki/Hayrack" title="Hayrack">hayracks</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">kozolci</i></span>).<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Four natural and cultural sites in Slovenia are on the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a> list. <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0kocjan_Caves" title="Škocjan Caves">Škocjan Caves</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Karst" title="Karst">karst</a> landscape are a protected site<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> as the old forests in the area of Goteniški Snežnik and Kočevski Rog in the SE Slovenia. The <a href="/wiki/Heritage_of_Mercury._Almad%C3%A9n_and_Idrija" title="Heritage of Mercury. Almadén and Idrija">Idrija Mercury mining site</a> is of world importance, as are the <a href="/wiki/Prehistoric_pile_dwellings_around_the_Alps" title="Prehistoric pile dwellings around the Alps">prehistoric pile dwellings</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Marsh" title="Ljubljana Marsh">Ljubljana Marsh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The most picturesque church for photographers is the medieval and Baroque building on <a href="/wiki/Lake_Bled#Bled_Island" title="Lake Bled">Bled Island</a>. Near <a href="/wiki/Postojna" title="Postojna">Postojna</a> there is a fortress called <a href="/wiki/Predjama_Castle" title="Predjama Castle">Predjama Castle</a>, half hidden in a cave. Museums in Ljubljana and elsewhere feature unique items such as the <a href="/wiki/Divje_Babe_Flute" class="mw-redirect" title="Divje Babe Flute">Divje Babe Flute</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Marshes_Wheel" title="Ljubljana Marshes Wheel">oldest wheel in the world</a>. Ljubljana has medieval, Baroque, Art Nouveau, and modern architecture. The architect <a href="/wiki/Ple%C4%8Dnik" class="mw-redirect" title="Plečnik">Plečnik</a>'s architecture and his innovative paths and bridges along the Ljubljanica are notable and on UNESCO tentative list.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_cuisine" title="Slovenian cuisine">Slovenian cuisine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_wine" title="Slovenian wine">Slovenian wine</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg/170px-Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg/255px-Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg/340px-Velika_no%C4%8D_-_jedila_hren_%C5%A1unka_pirhi_potica.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="3000" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Slovenian_potica" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian potica">Potica</a> as part of traditional Slovenian Easter breakfast</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenian cuisine is a mixture of Central European cuisine (especially <a href="/wiki/Austrian_cuisine" title="Austrian cuisine">Austrian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_cuisine" title="Hungarian cuisine">Hungarian</a>), <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_cuisine" title="Mediterranean cuisine">Mediterranean cuisine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Balkan_cuisine" title="Balkan cuisine">Balkan cuisine</a>. Historically, Slovenian cuisine was divided into town, farmhouse, cottage, castle, parsonage and monastic cuisines. Due to the variety of Slovenian cultural and natural landscapes, there are more than 40 distinct regional cuisines.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png/220px-Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="140" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png/330px-Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png/440px-Maribor_Zametovka_vine_and_daughter.png 2x" data-file-width="523" data-file-height="333" /></a><figcaption>The more-than-400-year-old Žametovka vine growing outside the Old Vine House in Maribor, Slovenia. To the right of the vine is a daughter vine taken from a cutting of the old vine.</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Ethnology" title="Ethnology">Ethnologically</a>, the most characteristic Slovene dishes were one-pot dishes, such as <i><a href="/wiki/Ri%C4%8Det" class="mw-redirect" title="Ričet">ričet</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Istrian_stew" title="Istrian stew">Istrian stew</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">jota</i></span>), <a href="/wiki/Minestrone" title="Minestrone">minestrone</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">mineštra</i></span>), and <i><a href="/wiki/%C5%BDganci" title="Žganci">žganci</a> </i>buckwheat spoonbread; in the <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">Prekmurje</a> region there is also <i><a href="/wiki/Bujta_repa" title="Bujta repa">bujta repa</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Prekmurska_gibanica" title="Prekmurska gibanica">prekmurska gibanica</a></i> pastry. <a href="/wiki/Prosciutto" title="Prosciutto">Prosciutto</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">pršut</i></span>) is a delicacy of the <a href="/wiki/Slovene_Littoral" title="Slovene Littoral">Slovene Littoral</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Nut_roll" title="Nut roll">nut roll</a> (<span title="Slovene-language text"><i lang="sl">potica</i></span>) has become a symbol of Slovenia, especially among the <a href="/wiki/American_Slovenes" class="mw-redirect" title="American Slovenes">Slovene diaspora in the United States</a>. <a href="/wiki/Soup" title="Soup">Soups</a> were added to the traditional one-pot meals and various kinds of porridge and stew only in relatively recent history.
</p><p>Each year since 2000, the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Roasted_Potato_Festival&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Roasted Potato Festival (page does not exist)">Roasted Potato Festival</a> has been organized by the <i>Society for the Recognition of Roasted Potatoes as a Distinct Dish</i>. Roasted potatoes, which have been traditionally served in most Slovenian families only on Sundays, have been depicted on a special edition of <a href="/wiki/Post_mark" class="mw-redirect" title="Post mark">post marks</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Post_of_Slovenia" title="Post of Slovenia">Post of Slovenia</a> in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> The best known sausage is <i><a href="/wiki/Kranjska_klobasa" class="mw-redirect" title="Kranjska klobasa">kranjska klobasa</a></i>. Slovenia is also the home of the world's <a href="/wiki/Old_vine" title="Old vine">oldest vine</a>, which is 400 years old.
</p><p>Slovenia has been awarded the <a href="/wiki/European_Region_of_Gastronomy" title="European Region of Gastronomy">European Region of Gastronomy</a> title for the year 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Dance">Dance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Dance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
