<p>Historically the most notable Slovenian ballet dancers and choreographers were <a href="/wiki/Pino_Mlakar" title="Pino Mlakar">Pino Mlakar</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> who in 1927 graduated from the <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_von_Laban" title="Rudolf von Laban">Rudolf Laban</a> Choreographic Institute, and there met his future wife, balerina <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pia_Mlakar&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pia Mlakar (page does not exist)">Maria Luiza Pia Beatrice Scholz</a>. Together they worked as a leading dancer and a choreographer in <a href="/wiki/Dessau" title="Dessau">Dessau</a>, <a href="/wiki/Z%C3%BCrich" title="Zürich">Zürich</a>, and State opera in <a href="/wiki/M%C3%BCnchen" class="mw-redirect" title="München">München</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Opera_si_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Opera_si-270">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup> Their plan to build a Slovenian dance centre at <a href="/wiki/Ro%C5%BEnik_Hill" class="mw-redirect" title="Rožnik Hill">Rožnik Hill</a> after the World War II was supported by the minister of culture, Ferdo Kozak, but was cancelled by his successor.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> Pino Mlakar was also a full professor at the <a href="/wiki/Academy_for_Theatre,_Radio,_Film_and_Television" class="mw-redirect" title="Academy for Theatre, Radio, Film and Television">Academy for Theatre, Radio, Film and Television</a> (AGRFT) of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Ljubljana" title="University of Ljubljana">University of Ljubljana</a>. A <a href="/wiki/Mary_Wigman" title="Mary Wigman">Mary Wigman</a> <a href="/wiki/Modern_dance" title="Modern dance">modern dance</a> school was founded in the 1930s by her student, <a href="/wiki/Meta_Vidmar" title="Meta Vidmar">Meta Vidmar</a>, in Ljubljana.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Festivals.2C_book_fairs.2C_and_other_events"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Festivals,_book_fairs,_and_other_events">Festivals, book fairs, and other events</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=53" title="Edit section: Festivals, book fairs, and other events">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>A number of music, theater, film, book, and children's festivals take place in Slovenia each year, including the music festivals <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Summer_Festival" title="Ljubljana Summer Festival">Ljubljana Summer Festival</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lent_Festival" title="Lent Festival">Lent Festival</a>, the stand-up comedy <a href="/wiki/Punch_Festival" title="Punch Festival">Punch Festival</a>, the children's <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pippi_Longstocking_Festival&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pippi Longstocking Festival (page does not exist)">Pippi Longstocking Festival</a>, and the book festivals <a href="/wiki/Slovene_book_fair" title="Slovene book fair">Slovene book fair</a> and <a href="/wiki/Frankfurt_after_the_Frankfurt" class="mw-redirect" title="Frankfurt after the Frankfurt">Frankfurt after the Frankfurt</a>.
</p><p>The most notable music festival of Slovene music was historically the <a href="/wiki/Slovenska_popevka" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenska popevka">Slovenska popevka</a> festival.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup> Between 1981 and 2000 the <a href="/wiki/Novi_Rock" title="Novi Rock">Novi Rock</a> festival was notable for bringing rock music across <a href="/wiki/Iron_curtain" class="mw-redirect" title="Iron curtain">Iron curtain</a> from the West to the Slovenian and then Yugoslav audience. The long tradition of jazz festivals in <a href="/wiki/Titoism" title="Titoism">Titoist</a> Yugoslavia began with the Ljubljana Jazz Festival which has been held annually in Slovenia since 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film">Film</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=54" title="Edit section: Film">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Slovene film actors and actresses historically include <a href="/wiki/Ida_Kravanja" class="mw-redirect" title="Ida Kravanja">Ida Kravanja</a>, who played her roles as <i>Ita Rina</i> in the early European films, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Metka_Bu%C4%8Dar&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Metka Bučar (page does not exist)">Metka Bučar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> After WW II, one of the most notable film actors was <a href="/wiki/Polde_Bibi%C4%8D" title="Polde Bibič">Polde Bibič</a>, who played a number of roles in many films that were well received in Slovenia, and also performed in television and radio drama.<sup id="cite_ref-Bibic_in_memoriam_276-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bibic_in_memoriam-276">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Feature film and short film production in Slovenia historically includes <a href="/wiki/Karol_Grossmann" title="Karol Grossmann">Karol Grossmann</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franti%C5%A1ek_%C4%8Cap" class="mw-redirect" title="František Čap">František Čap</a>, <a href="/wiki/France_%C5%A0tiglic" title="France Štiglic">France Štiglic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Igor_Pretnar" title="Igor Pretnar">Igor Pretnar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jo%C5%BEe_Poga%C4%8Dnik" title="Jože Pogačnik">Jože Pogačnik</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Peter_Zobec&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Peter Zobec (page does not exist)">Peter Zobec</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matja%C5%BE_Klop%C4%8Di%C4%8D" title="Matjaž Klopčič">Matjaž Klopčič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bo%C5%A1tjan_Hladnik" title="Boštjan Hladnik">Boštjan Hladnik</a>, <a href="/wiki/Du%C5%A1an_Jovanovi%C4%87_(theatre_director)" title="Dušan Jovanović (theatre director)">Dušan Jovanović</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vitan_Mal" title="Vitan Mal">Vitan Mal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franci_Slak" title="Franci Slak">Franci Slak</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Karpo_Godina" title="Karpo Godina">Karpo Godina</a> as its most established filmmakers. Contemporary film directors <a href="/wiki/Filip_Robar_-_Dorin" class="mw-redirect" title="Filip Robar - Dorin">Filip Robar - Dorin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Cvitkovi%C4%8D" title="Jan Cvitkovič">Jan Cvitkovič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Damjan_Kozole" title="Damjan Kozole">Damjan Kozole</a>, <a href="/wiki/Janez_Lapajne" title="Janez Lapajne">Janez Lapajne</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mitja_Okorn" title="Mitja Okorn">Mitja Okorn</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Marko_Naber%C5%A1nik" title="Marko Naberšnik">Marko Naberšnik</a> are among the representatives of the so-called "Renaissance of Slovenian cinema". Slovene screenwriters, who are not film directors, include <a href="/w/index.php?title=Sa%C5%A1a_Vuga&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Saša Vuga (page does not exist)">Saša Vuga</a> and <a href="/wiki/Miha_Mazzini" title="Miha Mazzini">Miha Mazzini</a>. Women film directors include <a href="/w/index.php?title=Polona_Sepe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Polona Sepe (page does not exist)">Polona Sepe</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Hanna_A._W._Slak&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Hanna A. W. Slak (page does not exist)">Hanna A. W. Slak</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Maja_Weiss&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Maja Weiss (page does not exist)">Maja Weiss</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=55" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_Slovene_writers_and_poets_in_Hungary" title="List of Slovene writers and poets in Hungary">List of Slovene writers and poets in Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_Slovenian_women_writers" title="List of Slovenian women writers">List of Slovenian women writers</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg/170px-Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="256" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg/255px-Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg/340px-Pre%C5%A1ern-Goldenstein.jpg 2x" data-file-width="680" data-file-height="1025" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/France_Pre%C5%A1eren" title="France Prešeren">France Prešeren</a>, best-known Slovenian poet</figcaption></figure>
<p>History of <a href="/wiki/Slovene_literature" title="Slovene literature">Slovene literature</a> began in the 16th century with <a href="/wiki/Primo%C5%BE_Trubar" title="Primož Trubar">Primož Trubar</a> and <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation_in_the_Slovene_Lands" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation in the Slovene Lands">other Protestant Reformers</a>. Poetry in <a href="/wiki/Slovene_language" title="Slovene language">Slovene</a> achieved its highest level with the <a href="/wiki/Romantic_poetry" title="Romantic poetry">Romantic</a> poet <a href="/wiki/France_Pre%C5%A1eren" title="France Prešeren">France Prešeren</a>. In the 20th century, Slovene literary fiction went through several periods: the beginning of the century was marked by the authors of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovene_Modernism&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Slovene Modernism (page does not exist)">Slovene Modernism</a>, with the most influential Slovene writer and playwright, <a href="/wiki/Ivan_Cankar" title="Ivan Cankar">Ivan Cankar</a>; it was then followed by <a href="/wiki/Expressionism" title="Expressionism">expressionism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Sre%C4%8Dko_Kosovel" title="Srečko Kosovel">Srečko Kosovel</a>), avantgardism (<a href="/wiki/Anton_Podbev%C5%A1ek" title="Anton Podbevšek">Anton Podbevšek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ferdo_Delak" title="Ferdo Delak">Ferdo Delak</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Social_realism" title="Social realism">social realism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Ciril_Kosma%C4%8D" title="Ciril Kosmač">Ciril Kosmač</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pre%C5%BEihov_Voranc" title="Prežihov Voranc">Prežihov Voranc</a>) before World War II, the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Poetry_of_resistance_and_revolution&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Poetry of resistance and revolution (page does not exist)">poetry of resistance and revolution</a> (<a href="/wiki/Karel_Destovnik_Kajuh" class="mw-redirect" title="Karel Destovnik Kajuh">Karel Destovnik Kajuh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matej_Bor" title="Matej Bor">Matej Bor</a>) during the war, and <a href="/wiki/Intimism_(Slovene_poetry)" class="mw-redirect" title="Intimism (Slovene poetry)">intimism</a> (<a href="/w/index.php?title=Poems_of_the_Four&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Poems of the Four (page does not exist)">Poems of the Four</a>, 1953), <a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenian_post-war_modernism&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Slovenian post-war modernism (page does not exist)">post-war modernism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Edvard_Kocbek" title="Edvard Kocbek">Edvard Kocbek</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Existentialism" title="Existentialism">existentialism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Dane_Zajc" title="Dane Zajc">Dane Zajc</a>) after the war.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Postmodernism" title="Postmodernism">Postmodernist</a> authors include <a href="/wiki/Boris_A._Novak" title="Boris A. Novak">Boris A. Novak</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marko_Kravos" title="Marko Kravos">Marko Kravos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Drago_Jan%C4%8Dar" title="Drago Jančar">Drago Jančar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Evald_Flisar" title="Evald Flisar">Evald Flisar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Toma%C5%BE_%C5%A0alamun" title="Tomaž Šalamun">Tomaž Šalamun</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Brina_Svit" title="Brina Svit">Brina Svit</a>. Among the post-1990 authors best known are <a href="/wiki/Ale%C5%A1_Debeljak" title="Aleš Debeljak">Aleš Debeljak</a>, <a href="/wiki/Miha_Mazzini" title="Miha Mazzini">Miha Mazzini</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Alojz_Ihan" title="Alojz Ihan">Alojz Ihan</a>. There are several <a href="/wiki/Literary_magazines" class="mw-redirect" title="Literary magazines">literary magazines</a> that publish Slovene prose, poetry, essays, and local literary criticism.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Today, notable authors include <a href="/wiki/Slavoj_%C5%BDi%C5%BEek" title="Slavoj Žižek">Slavoj Žižek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mladen_Dolar" title="Mladen Dolar">Mladen Dolar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alenka_Zupan%C4%8Di%C4%8D" title="Alenka Zupančič">Alenka Zupančič</a> as well as <a href="/wiki/Boris_Pahor" title="Boris Pahor">Boris Pahor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=56" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Slovenia" title="Music of Slovenia">Music of Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg/170px-Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="218" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg/255px-Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg/340px-Pre%C4%8Drtana_kitica_Zdravljice.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3696" data-file-height="4737" /></a><figcaption>"<a href="/wiki/Zdravljica" title="Zdravljica">Zdravljica</a>" (<i>A Toast</i>; part) with rejection mark from <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian</a> censorship (due to potential revolutionary content); the music of Zdravljica is now the Slovenian national <a href="/wiki/Anthem" title="Anthem">anthem</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Music_of_Slovenia" title="Music of Slovenia">Music of Slovenia</a> historically includes numerous musicians and composers, such as the <a href="/wiki/Renaissance" title="Renaissance">Renaissance</a> composer <a href="/wiki/Jacobus_Gallus" title="Jacobus Gallus">Jacobus Gallus</a>, who greatly influenced Central European classical music, the Baroque composer <a href="/wiki/Joannes_Baptista_Dolar" title="Joannes Baptista Dolar">Joannes Baptista Dolar</a>, and the violin virtuoso <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Tartini" title="Giuseppe Tartini">Giuseppe Tartini</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The first Slovenian hymnal, <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eni_Psalmi&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Eni Psalmi (page does not exist)">Eni Psalmi</a></i>, was published in 1567. This period saw the rise of musicians like <a href="/wiki/Jacobus_Gallus" title="Jacobus Gallus">Jacobus Gallus</a> and <a href="/wiki/George_Slatkonia" title="George Slatkonia">George Slatkonia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> In 1701, <a href="/wiki/Johann_Berthold_von_H%C3%B6ffer" title="Johann Berthold von Höffer">Johann Berthold von Höffer</a> founded the <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Philharmonics" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian Philharmonics">Academia Philharmonicorum Labacensis</a>, as one of the oldest such institutions in Europe, based on Italian models.<sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> Composers of Slovenian <a href="/wiki/Lieder" class="mw-redirect" title="Lieder">Lieder</a> and <a href="/wiki/Art_song" title="Art song">art songs</a> include <a href="/wiki/Emil_Adami%C4%8D" title="Emil Adamič">Emil Adamič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fran_Gerbi%C4%8D" title="Fran Gerbič">Fran Gerbič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alojz_Ger%C5%BEini%C4%8D" title="Alojz Geržinič">Alojz Geržinič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Ipavec" title="Benjamin Ipavec">Benjamin Ipavec</a>, <a href="/wiki/Davorin_Jenko" title="Davorin Jenko">Davorin Jenko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anton_Lajovic" title="Anton Lajovic">Anton Lajovic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kamilo_Ma%C5%A1ek" title="Kamilo Mašek">Kamilo Mašek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Josip_Pav%C4%8Di%C4%8D" title="Josip Pavčič">Josip Pavčič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zorko_Prelovec" title="Zorko Prelovec">Zorko Prelovec</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lucijan_Marija_%C5%A0kerjanc" title="Lucijan Marija Škerjanc">Lucijan Marija Škerjanc</a>.
</p><p>In the early 20th century, <a href="/wiki/Impressionism" title="Impressionism">impressionism</a> was spreading across Slovenia, which soon produced composers <a href="/wiki/Marij_Kogoj" title="Marij Kogoj">Marij Kogoj</a> and <a href="/wiki/Slavko_Osterc" title="Slavko Osterc">Slavko Osterc</a>. Avant-garde <a href="/wiki/European_classical_music" class="mw-redirect" title="European classical music">classical music</a> arose in Slovenia in the 1960s, largely due to the work of <a href="/wiki/Uro%C5%A1_Krek" title="Uroš Krek">Uroš Krek</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Dane_Skerl&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Dane Skerl (page does not exist)">Dane Škerl</a>, <a href="/wiki/Primoz_Ramovs" class="mw-redirect" title="Primoz Ramovs">Primož Ramovš</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ivo_Petric" class="mw-redirect" title="Ivo Petric">Ivo Petrić</a>, who also conducted the <a href="/wiki/Slavko_Osterc_Ensemble" title="Slavko Osterc Ensemble">Slavko Osterc Ensemble</a>. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Jakob_Jez&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Jakob Jez (page does not exist)">Jakob Jež</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Darijan_Bo%C5%BEi%C4%8D&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Darijan Božič (page does not exist)">Darijan Božič</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Lojze_Lebic&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Lojze Lebic (page does not exist)">Lojze Lebič</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vinko_Globokar" title="Vinko Globokar">Vinko Globokar</a> have since composed enduring works, especially Globokar's <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=L%27Armonia_(opera)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="L&#39;Armonia (opera) (page does not exist)">L'Armonia</a></i>, an opera.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Modern composers include <a href="/w/index.php?title=Uros_Rojko&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Uros Rojko (page does not exist)">Uroš Rojko</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Tomaz_Svete&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tomaz Svete (page does not exist)">Tomaž Svete</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Brina_Jez-Brezavscek&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Brina Jez-Brezavscek (page does not exist)">Brina Jež-Brezavšček</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bo%C5%BEidar_Kantu%C5%A1er" title="Božidar Kantušer">Božidar Kantušer</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Aldo_Kumar&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Aldo Kumar (page does not exist)">Aldo Kumar</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Slovene_National_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovene National Opera and Ballet Theatre">Slovene National Opera and Ballet Theatre</a> serves as the national opera and ballet house. The <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_Philharmonics" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian Philharmonics">Slovenian Philharmonics</a>, established in 1701 as part of <a href="/wiki/Academia_operosorum_Labacensis" class="mw-redirect" title="Academia operosorum Labacensis">Academia operosorum Labacensis</a>, is among the oldest such institutions in Europe.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Slak_lojze.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Slak_lojze.jpg/220px-Slak_lojze.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Slak_lojze.jpg/330px-Slak_lojze.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Slak_lojze.jpg/440px-Slak_lojze.jpg 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="567" /></a><figcaption>Folk musician <a href="/wiki/Lojze_Slak" title="Lojze Slak">Lojze Slak</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Harmony" title="Harmony">Harmony</a> singing is a deep rooted tradition in Slovenia, and is at least three-part singing (four voices), while in some regions even up to eight-part singing (nine voices). Slovenian folk songs, thus, usually resounds soft and harmonious, and are very seldom in minor. Traditional Slovenian folk music is performed on Styrian harmonica (the oldest type of accordion), fiddle, clarinet, <a href="/wiki/Zither" title="Zither">zithers</a>, flute, and by brass bands of alpine type. In <a href="/wiki/Prekmurje" title="Prekmurje">eastern Slovenia</a>, fiddle and cimbalon bands are called <a href="/w/index.php?title=Velike_goslarije&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Velike goslarije (page does not exist)">velike goslarije</a>. From 1952 on, the <a href="/wiki/Slavko_Avsenik" title="Slavko Avsenik">Slavko Avsenik</a>'s band began to appear in broadcasts, movies, and concerts all over the <a href="/wiki/West_Germany" title="West Germany">West Germany</a>, inventing the original "<a href="/wiki/Gorenjska" class="mw-redirect" title="Gorenjska">Oberkrainer</a>" country sound. The band produced nearly 1000 original compositions, an integral part of the <a href="/wiki/Slovenian-style_polka" title="Slovenian-style polka">Slovenian-style polka</a> legacy. Many musicians followed Avsenik's steps, including <a href="/wiki/Lojze_Slak" title="Lojze Slak">Lojze Slak</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg/220px-LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="129" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg/330px-LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg/440px-LAIBACH_Press_Photo_2011.jpg 2x" data-file-width="550" data-file-height="322" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Industrial_music" title="Industrial music">industrial</a> group <a href="/wiki/Laibach_(band)" class="mw-redirect" title="Laibach (band)">Laibach</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Among pop, rock, industrial, and indie musicians the most popular in Slovenia include <a href="/wiki/Industrial_music" title="Industrial music">industrial music</a> group <a href="/wiki/Laibach_(band)" class="mw-redirect" title="Laibach (band)">Laibach</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/Siddharta_(band)" title="Siddharta (band)">Siddharta</a>, a rock band formed in 1995. <a href="/wiki/Perpetuum_Jazzile" title="Perpetuum Jazzile">Perpetuum Jazzile</a> is the group from Slovenia that is internationally most listened online, with more than 23 million views for the official <a href="/wiki/A_cappella" title="A cappella">a cappella</a> "<a href="/wiki/Africa_(Toto_song)" title="Africa (Toto song)">Africa</a>" video since its publishing on YouTube in May 2009 (through January 2023).<sup id="cite_ref-youtube_africa_285-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-youtube_africa-285">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> Other Slovenian bands include a historically <a href="/wiki/Progressive_rock" title="Progressive rock">progressive rock</a> ones that were also popular in <a href="/wiki/Titoism" title="Titoism">Titoist</a> Yugoslavia, such as <a href="/wiki/Buldo%C5%BEer" title="Buldožer">Buldožer</a> and <a href="/wiki/La%C4%8Dni_Franz" title="Lačni Franz">Lačni Franz</a>, which inspired later <a href="/wiki/Comedy_rock" title="Comedy rock">comedy rock</a> bands including <a href="/wiki/Zmelkoow" title="Zmelkoow">Zmelkoow</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slon_in_Sade%C5%BE" title="Slon in Sadež">Slon in Sadež</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mi2_(rock_band)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mi2 (rock band)">Mi2</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup> With exception of <a href="/wiki/Terrafolk" title="Terrafolk">Terrafolk</a> that made appearances worldwide, other bands, such as <a href="/wiki/Avtomobili" title="Avtomobili">Avtomobili</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zakloni%C5%A1%C4%8De_Prepeva" class="mw-redirect" title="Zaklonišče Prepeva">Zaklonišče Prepeva</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0ank_Rock" title="Šank Rock">Šank Rock</a>, <a href="/wiki/Big_Foot_Mama" title="Big Foot Mama">Big Foot Mama</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dan_D" title="Dan D">Dan D</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Zablujena_generacija" title="Zablujena generacija">Zablujena generacija</a>, are mostly unknown outside the country. Slovenian metal bands include <a href="/w/index.php?title=Noctiferia&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Noctiferia (page does not exist)">Noctiferia</a> (<a href="/wiki/Death_metal" title="Death metal">death metal</a>), <a href="/wiki/Negligence_(band)" title="Negligence (band)">Negligence</a> (<a href="/wiki/Thrash_metal" title="Thrash metal">thrash metal</a>), <a href="/wiki/Naio_Ssaion" title="Naio Ssaion">Naio Ssaion</a> (<a href="/wiki/Gothic_metal" title="Gothic metal">gothic metal</a>), and Within Destruction (<a href="/wiki/Deathcore" title="Deathcore">deathcore</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup>
Slovenian post-WWII singer-songwriters include <a href="/wiki/Frane_Mil%C4%8Dinski" title="Frane Milčinski">Frane Milčinski</a>, <a href="/wiki/Toma%C5%BE_Pengov" title="Tomaž Pengov">Tomaž Pengov</a> whose 1973 album <i>Odpotovanja</i> is considered to be the first singer-songwriter album in <a href="/wiki/Former_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Former Yugoslavia">former Yugoslavia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-288" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-288">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Toma%C5%BE_Domicelj&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tomaž Domicelj (page does not exist)">Tomaž Domicelj</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Marko_Brecelj&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Marko Brecelj (page does not exist)">Marko Brecelj</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Andrej_%C5%A0ifrer&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Andrej Šifrer (page does not exist)">Andrej Šifrer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eva_Sr%C5%A1en" title="Eva Sršen">Eva Sršen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Neca_Falk" title="Neca Falk">Neca Falk</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jani_Kova%C4%8Di%C4%8D" title="Jani Kovačič">Jani Kovačič</a>. After 1990, <a href="/wiki/Adi_Smolar" title="Adi Smolar">Adi Smolar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iztok_Mlakar" title="Iztok Mlakar">Iztok Mlakar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vita_Mavri%C4%8D" title="Vita Mavrič">Vita Mavrič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vlado_Kreslin" title="Vlado Kreslin">Vlado Kreslin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zoran_Predin" title="Zoran Predin">Zoran Predin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peter_Lov%C5%A1in" title="Peter Lovšin">Peter Lovšin</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Magnifico_(musician)" title="Magnifico (musician)">Magnifico</a> have been popular in Slovenia, as well. In the 21st century, there have been many successful artists from Slovenia. They include country musician <a href="/wiki/ManuElla" title="ManuElla">Manu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision</a> finalists <a href="/wiki/Zalagasper" title="Zalagasper">zalagasper</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nika_Zorjan" title="Nika Zorjan">Nika Zorjan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Omar_Naber" title="Omar Naber">Omar Naber</a> and <a href="/wiki/Raiven" title="Raiven">Raiven</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Theatre">Theatre</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=57" title="Edit section: Theatre">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana,_2011.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg/220px-Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="102" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg/330px-Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg/440px-Drama_theatre_in_Ljubljana%2C_2011.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5644" data-file-height="2611" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Slovene_National_Theatre,_Ljubljana" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovene National Theatre, Ljubljana">National Theatre</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Theater has a rich tradition in Slovenia, starting with the 1867 first ever Slovene-language drama performance.<sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup> In addition to the main houses, which include <a href="/wiki/Slovene_National_Theatre,_Ljubljana" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovene National Theatre, Ljubljana">Slovene National Theatre, Ljubljana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maribor_National_Drama_Theatre" class="mw-redirect" title="Maribor National Drama Theatre">Maribor National Drama Theatre</a>, a number of small producers are active in Slovenia, including <a href="/wiki/Physical_theatre" title="Physical theatre">physical theatre</a> (e.g. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Betontanc&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Betontanc (page does not exist)">Betontanc</a>), <a href="/wiki/Street_theatre" title="Street theatre">street theatre</a> (e.g. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ana_Monr%C3%B3_Theatre&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ana Monró Theatre (page does not exist)">Ana Monró Theatre</a>), <a href="/wiki/Theatresports" title="Theatresports">theatresports</a> championship <a href="/wiki/Impro_League" title="Impro League">Impro League</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Improvisational_theatre" title="Improvisational theatre">improvisational theatre</a> (e.g. <a href="/wiki/IGLU_Theatre" title="IGLU Theatre">IGLU Theatre</a>). A popular form is <a href="/wiki/Puppetry" title="Puppetry">puppetry</a>, mainly performed in the <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana_Puppet_Theatre" title="Ljubljana Puppet Theatre">Ljubljana Puppet Theatre</a>.
</p>
<h3><span id="Visual_arts.2C_architecture_and_design"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Visual_arts,_architecture_and_design">Visual arts, architecture and design</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=58" title="Edit section: Visual arts, architecture and design">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Slovenia's visual arts, architecture, and design are shaped by a number of architects, designers, painters, sculptors, photographers, graphics artists, as well as comics, illustration and conceptual artists. Two significant prestigious institutions exhibiting works of Slovene visual artists are the <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Slovenia" title="National Gallery of Slovenia">National Gallery of Slovenia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art,_Ljubljana" class="mw-redirect" title="Museum of Modern Art, Ljubljana">Museum of Modern Art</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Modern_architecture" title="Modern architecture">Modern architecture</a> in Slovenia was introduced by <a href="/wiki/Max_Fabiani" title="Max Fabiani">Max Fabiani</a>, and in the mid-war period, <a href="/wiki/Jo%C5%BEe_Ple%C4%8Dnik" title="Jože Plečnik">Jože Plečnik</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ivan_Vurnik" title="Ivan Vurnik">Ivan Vurnik</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Štravs2011_290-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Štravs2011-290">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup> In the second half of the 20th century, the national and universal style were merged by the architects <a href="/wiki/Edvard_Ravnikar" title="Edvard Ravnikar">Edvard Ravnikar</a> and first generation of his students: Milan Mihelič, Stanko Kristl, Savin Sever. The next generation is mainly still active, including <a href="/wiki/Marko_Mu%C5%A1i%C4%8D" title="Marko Mušič">Marko Mušič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vojteh_Ravnikar" title="Vojteh Ravnikar">Vojteh Ravnikar</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Jurij_Kobe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Jurij Kobe (page does not exist)">Jurij Kobe</a>. Selected works of Jože Plečnik which shaped Ljubljana during the inter-war period were inscribed on <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>'s list of <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Sites</a> in 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-plecnik_whc_291-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-plecnik_whc-291">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A number of <a href="/wiki/Conceptual_art" title="Conceptual art">conceptual visual art</a> groups formed, including <a href="/w/index.php?title=OHO_(art_group)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="OHO (art group) (page does not exist)">OHO</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Group_69&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Group 69 (page does not exist)">Group 69</a>, and <a href="/wiki/IRWIN" title="IRWIN">IRWIN</a>. Nowadays, the Slovene visual arts are diverse, based on tradition, reflect the influence of neighboring nations and are intertwined with modern European movements.<sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Internationally most notable Slovenian design items include the 1952 <a href="/wiki/Rex_(chair)" title="Rex (chair)">Rex</a> chair, a <a href="/wiki/Scandinavian_design" title="Scandinavian design">Scandinavian design</a>-inspired wooden chair, by interior designer <a href="/w/index.php?title=Niko_Kralj&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Niko Kralj (page does not exist)">Niko Kralj</a> that was given in 2012 a permanent place in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Designmuseum&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Designmuseum (page does not exist)">Designmuseum</a>, <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a>, the largest museum of design in Scandinavia, and is included in the collection of the <a href="/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art,_New_York" class="mw-redirect" title="Museum of Modern Art, New York">Museum of Modern Art</a> <a href="/wiki/MOMA" class="mw-redirect" title="MOMA">MOMA</a> in New York City, as well. An <a href="/wiki/Industrial_design" title="Industrial design">industrial design</a> item that has changed the international ski industry is <a href="/wiki/Elan_SCX" title="Elan SCX">Elan SCX</a> by <a href="/wiki/Elan_(company)" title="Elan (company)">Elan</a> company.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg/170px-V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg/255px-V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg/340px-V._Vodnik_monument_Ljubljana_front_side_2008-03.jpg 2x" data-file-width="683" data-file-height="1024" /></a><figcaption>The sculpture of the poet <a href="/wiki/Valentin_Vodnik" title="Valentin Vodnik">Valentin Vodnik</a> was created by Alojz Gangl in 1889 as part of <a href="/wiki/Vodnik_Monument" title="Vodnik Monument">Vodnik Monument</a>, the first Slovene national monument.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The renewal of Slovene sculpture begun with <a href="/w/index.php?title=Alojz_Gangl&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Alojz Gangl (page does not exist)">Alojz Gangl</a> who created sculptures for the public monuments of the <a href="/wiki/Carniola" title="Carniola">Carniolan</a> polymath <a href="/wiki/Johann_Weikhard_von_Valvasor" title="Johann Weikhard von Valvasor">Johann Weikhard von Valvasor</a> and <a href="/wiki/Valentin_Vodnik" title="Valentin Vodnik">Valentin Vodnik</a>, the first Slovene poet and journalist, as well as <i>The Genius of the Theatre</i> and other statues for the <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_National_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian National Opera and Ballet Theatre">Slovenian National Opera and Ballet Theatre</a> building.<sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> The development of sculpture after World War II was led by a number of artists, including brothers <a href="/wiki/Boris_Kalin" title="Boris Kalin">Boris</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Zdenko_Kalin&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Zdenko Kalin (page does not exist)">Zdenko Kalin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jakob_Savin%C5%A1ek" title="Jakob Savinšek">Jakob Savinšek</a> stayed with figural art. Younger sculptors, for example <a href="/w/index.php?title=Janez_Boljka&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Janez Boljka (page does not exist)">Janez Boljka</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Drago_Tr%C5%A1ar&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Drago Tršar (page does not exist)">Drago Tršar</a> and particularly <a href="/w/index.php?title=Slavko_Tihec&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Slavko Tihec (page does not exist)">Slavko Tihec</a>, moved towards <a href="/wiki/Abstract_sculpture" class="mw-redirect" title="Abstract sculpture">abstract</a> forms. <a href="/wiki/Jakov_Brdar" title="Jakov Brdar">Jakov Brdar</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mirsad_Begi%C4%87&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mirsad Begić (page does not exist)">Mirsad Begić</a> returned to human figures.
</p><p>During World War II, numerous graphics were created by <a href="/wiki/Bo%C5%BEidar_Jakac" title="Božidar Jakac">Božidar Jakac</a>, who helped establish the post-war <a href="/wiki/Academy_of_Visual_Arts_and_Design,_Ljubljana" class="mw-redirect" title="Academy of Visual Arts and Design, Ljubljana">Academy of Visual Arts</a> in Ljubljana.
</p><p>In 1917 <a href="/wiki/Hinko_Smrekar" title="Hinko Smrekar">Hinko Smrekar</a> illustrated <a href="/wiki/Fran_Levstik" title="Fran Levstik">Fran Levstik</a>'s book about the well-known Slovene folk hero <a href="/wiki/Martin_Krpan" title="Martin Krpan">Martin Krpan</a>. The children's books illustrators include a number of women illustrators, such as <a href="/wiki/Marlenka_Stupica" title="Marlenka Stupica">Marlenka Stupica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marija_Lucija_Stupica" title="Marija Lucija Stupica">Marija Lucija Stupica</a>, <a href="/wiki/An%C4%8Dka_Go%C5%A1nik_Godec" title="Ančka Gošnik Godec">Ančka Gošnik Godec</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marjanca_Jemec_Bo%C5%BEi%C4%8D" title="Marjanca Jemec Božič">Marjanca Jemec Božič</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jelka_Reichman" title="Jelka Reichman">Jelka Reichman</a>.
</p><p>Historically, painting and sculpture in Slovenia was in the late 18th and the 19th century marked by <a href="/wiki/Neoclassicism" title="Neoclassicism">Neoclassicism</a> (<a href="/w/index.php?title=Matev%C5%BE_Langus&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Matevž Langus (page does not exist)">Matevž Langus</a>), <a href="/wiki/Biedermeier#Visual_arts" title="Biedermeier">Biedermeier</a> (<a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Tominz" title="Giuseppe Tominz">Giuseppe Tominz</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Romantic_visual_arts" class="mw-redirect" title="Romantic visual arts">Romanticism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Michael_Stroy" title="Michael Stroy">Michael Stroy</a>). The first art exhibition in Slovenia was organized in the late 19th century by <a href="/wiki/Ivana_Kobilca" title="Ivana Kobilca">Ivana Kobilca</a>. <a href="/wiki/Impressionism_(arts)" class="mw-redirect" title="Impressionism (arts)">Impressionist</a> artists include <a href="/wiki/Matej_Sternen" title="Matej Sternen">Matej Sternen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matija_Jama" title="Matija Jama">Matija Jama</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rihard_Jakopi%C4%8D" title="Rihard Jakopič">Rihard Jakopič</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ivan_Grohar" title="Ivan Grohar">Ivan Grohar</a> whose <i>The Sower</i> (Slovene: Sejalec) was depicted on the €0.05 <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_euro_coins" title="Slovenian euro coins">Slovenian euro coins</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Franc_Berneker" title="Franc Berneker">Franc Berneker</a>, who introduced impressionism to Slovenia. <a href="/wiki/Expressionism" title="Expressionism">Espressionist</a> painters include <a href="/wiki/Veno_Pilon" title="Veno Pilon">Veno Pilon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tone_Kralj" title="Tone Kralj">Tone Kralj</a> whose picture book, reprinted thirteen times, is now the most recognisable image of Martin Krpan.<sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup> Some of the best known painters in the second half of the 20th century were <a href="/wiki/Zoran_Mu%C5%A1i%C4%8D" title="Zoran Mušič">Zoran Mušič</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabrijel_Stupica&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gabrijel Stupica (page does not exist)">Gabrijel Stupica</a> and <a href="/wiki/Marij_Pregelj" title="Marij Pregelj">Marij Pregelj</a>.
</p><p>In 1841, <a href="/wiki/Janez_Puhar" class="mw-redirect" title="Janez Puhar">Janez Puhar</a> invented a process for photography on glass, recognized on 17 June 1852 in Paris by the Académie Nationale Agricole, Manufacturière et Commerciale.<sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Gojmir_Anton_Kos" title="Gojmir Anton Kos">Gojmir Anton Kos</a> was a notable <a href="/wiki/Realism_(arts)" title="Realism (arts)">realist</a> painter and photographer between First World War and WW II.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=59" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Slovenia" title="Sport in Slovenia">Sport in Slovenia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg/170px-Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="321" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg/255px-Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg/340px-Tina_Maze_with_Olympic_silver_medal_2010.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1904" data-file-height="3600" /></a><figcaption>Alpine skier <a href="/wiki/Tina_Maze" title="Tina Maze">Tina Maze</a>, a double Olympic gold medalist and the overall winner of the 2012–13 World Cup season</figcaption></figure>
<p>Slovenia is a natural sports venue, with many Slovenians actively practicing sports.<sup id="cite_ref-SportSLO.SI_296-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SportSLO.SI-296">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup> A variety of sports are played in Slovenia on a professional level,<sup id="cite_ref-medjugorac4_297-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-medjugorac4-297">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup> with international successes in <a href="/wiki/Team_handball" class="mw-redirect" title="Team handball">handball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, association football, <a href="/wiki/Ice_hockey" title="Ice hockey">ice hockey</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rowing_(sport)" title="Rowing (sport)">rowing</a>, swimming, <a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">tennis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boxing" title="Boxing">boxing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Climbing" title="Climbing">climbing</a>, road cycling and <a href="/wiki/Athletics_(sport)" class="mw-redirect" title="Athletics (sport)">athletics</a>. Prior to World War II, <a href="/wiki/Gymnastics" title="Gymnastics">gymnastics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fencing" title="Fencing">fencing</a> used to be the most popular sports in Slovenia, with athletes like <a href="/wiki/Leon_%C5%A0tukelj" title="Leon Štukelj">Leon Štukelj</a> and <a href="/wiki/Miroslav_Cerar" title="Miroslav Cerar">Miroslav Cerar</a> gaining gold Olympic medals.<sup id="cite_ref-298" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-298">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> Association football gained popularity in the interwar period. After 1945, basketball, handball and volleyball have become popular among Slovenians, and from the mid-1970s onward, <a href="/wiki/Winter_sports" title="Winter sports">winter sports</a> have, as well. Since 1992, Slovenian sportspeople have won <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_at_the_Olympics" title="Slovenia at the Olympics">52 Olympic medals</a>, including twelve gold medals, and <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_at_the_Paralympics" title="Slovenia at the Paralympics">24 Paralympic medals</a> with four golds.<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-300" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-300">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Individual sports are also very popular in Slovenia, including tennis and <a href="/wiki/Mountaineering" title="Mountaineering">mountaineering</a>, which are two of the most widespread sporting activities in Slovenia. Several Slovenian <a href="/wiki/Extreme_sport" title="Extreme sport">extreme</a> and <a href="/wiki/Endurance_sports" class="mw-redirect" title="Endurance sports">endurance sportsmen</a> have gained an international reputation, including the mountaineer <a href="/wiki/Toma%C5%BE_Humar" title="Tomaž Humar">Tomaž Humar</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-301" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-301">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup> the mountain skier <a href="/wiki/Davo_Karni%C4%8Dar" title="Davo Karničar">Davo Karničar</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-302" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-302">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> the ultramarathon swimmer <a href="/wiki/Martin_Strel" title="Martin Strel">Martin Strel</a><sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup> and the ultracyclist <a href="/wiki/Jure_Robi%C4%8D" title="Jure Robič">Jure Robič</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-304" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-304">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> Past and current winter sports athletes include <a href="/wiki/Alpine_skiing" title="Alpine skiing">alpine skiers</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Mateja_Svet" title="Mateja Svet">Mateja Svet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bojan_Kri%C5%BEaj" title="Bojan Križaj">Bojan Križaj</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ilka_%C5%A0tuhec" title="Ilka Štuhec">Ilka Štuhec</a> and double Olympic gold medalist <a href="/wiki/Tina_Maze" title="Tina Maze">Tina Maze</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-306" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-306">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cross-country_skiing_(sport)" title="Cross-country skiing (sport)">cross-country skier</a> <a href="/wiki/Petra_Majdi%C4%8D" title="Petra Majdič">Petra Majdič</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-307" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-307">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Ski_jumping" title="Ski jumping">ski jumpers</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Primo%C5%BE_Peterka" title="Primož Peterka">Primož Peterka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Peter_Prevc" title="Peter Prevc">Peter Prevc</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-308" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-308">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup> Boxing has gained popularity since <a href="/wiki/Jan_Zaveck" title="Jan Zaveck">Jan Zaveck</a> won the <a href="/wiki/International_Boxing_Federation" title="International Boxing Federation">IBF</a> <a href="/wiki/List_of_welterweight_boxing_champions" class="mw-redirect" title="List of welterweight boxing champions">Welterweight World Champion</a> title in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-309" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-309">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In cycling, <a href="/wiki/Primo%C5%BE_Rogli%C4%8D" title="Primož Roglič">Primož Roglič</a> became the first Slovenian to win a <a href="/wiki/Grand_Tour_(cycling)" title="Grand Tour (cycling)">Grand Tour</a> when he won the <a href="/wiki/2019_Vuelta_a_Espa%C3%B1a" title="2019 Vuelta a España">2019 Vuelta a España</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-310" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-310">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tadej_Poga%C4%8Dar" title="Tadej Pogačar">Tadej Pogačar</a> won the <a href="/wiki/Tour_de_France" title="Tour de France">Tour de France</a>, the world's most competitive cycling race, in 2020<sup id="cite_ref-311" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-311">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup> and 2021.
Prominent team sports in Slovenia include football, basketball, handball, volleyball, and ice hockey. The <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_national_football_team" title="Slovenia national football team">men's national football team</a> has qualified for one <a href="/wiki/UEFA_European_Football_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="UEFA European Football Championship">European Championship</a> (2000) and two <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">World Cups</a> (2002 and 2010).<sup id="cite_ref-312" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-312">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> Of Slovenian clubs, <a href="/wiki/NK_Maribor" title="NK Maribor">NK Maribor</a> played three times in the group stages of the <a href="/wiki/UEFA_Champions_League" title="UEFA Champions League">UEFA Champions League</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-313" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-313">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian national basketball team">men's national basketball team</a> has participated at 14 <a href="/wiki/EuroBasket" title="EuroBasket">EuroBaskets</a>, winning the gold medal in the 2017 edition,<sup id="cite_ref-314" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-314">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup> and at four <a href="/wiki/FIBA_World_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="FIBA World Championship">FIBA World Championships</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-315" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-315">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia also hosted the <a href="/wiki/EuroBasket_2013" title="EuroBasket 2013">EuroBasket 2013</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-316" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-316">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_men%27s_national_handball_team" title="Slovenia men&#39;s national handball team">men's national handball team</a> has qualified for three Olympics, ten IHF <a href="/wiki/IHF_World_Men%27s_Handball_Championship" title="IHF World Men&#39;s Handball Championship">World Championships</a>, including their third-place finish in 2017,<sup id="cite_ref-317" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-317">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> and thirteen <a href="/wiki/European_Men%27s_Handball_Championship" title="European Men&#39;s Handball Championship">European Championships</a>. Slovenia was the hosts of the <a href="/wiki/2004_European_Men%27s_Handball_Championship" title="2004 European Men&#39;s Handball Championship">2004 European Championship</a>, where the national team won the silver medal.<sup id="cite_ref-2004handball_318-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2004handball-318">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup> Slovenia's most prominent handball team, <a href="/wiki/RK_Celje" title="RK Celje">RK Celje</a>, won the <a href="/wiki/EHF_Champions_League" title="EHF Champions League">EHF Champions League</a> in the 2003–04 season.<sup id="cite_ref-2004handball_318-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2004handball-318">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup> In women's handball, <a href="/wiki/RK_Krim" title="RK Krim">RK Krim</a> won the <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_EHF_Champions_League" title="Women&#39;s EHF Champions League">Champions League</a> in 2001 and 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-319" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-319">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_men%27s_national_volleyball_team" title="Slovenia men&#39;s national volleyball team">men's national volleyball team</a> has won three silver medals at the <a href="/wiki/Men%27s_European_Volleyball_Championship" title="Men&#39;s European Volleyball Championship">European Volleyball Championship</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-320" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-320">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup> and finished fourth at the <a href="/wiki/2022_FIVB_Volleyball_Men%27s_World_Championship" title="2022 FIVB Volleyball Men&#39;s World Championship">2022 World Championship</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-321" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-321">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Slovenia_men%27s_national_ice_hockey_team" title="Slovenia men&#39;s national ice hockey team">national ice hockey team</a> has played at 29 <a href="/wiki/Ice_Hockey_World_Championships" title="Ice Hockey World Championships">Ice Hockey World Championships</a> (with 10 appearances in top division<sup id="cite_ref-322" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-322">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup>).
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=60" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1132942124">.mw-parser-output .portalbox{padding:0;margin:0.5em 0;display:table;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:175px;list-style:none}.mw-parser-output .portalborder{border:solid #aaa 1px;padding:0.1em;background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-entry{display:table-row;font-size:85%;line-height:110%;height:1.9em;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-image{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-link{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em 0.2em 0.2em 0.3em;vertical-align:middle}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .portalleft{clear:left;float:left;margin:0.5em 1em 0.5em 0}.mw-parser-output .portalright{clear:right;float:right;margin:0.5em 0 0.5em 1em}}</style><ul role="navigation" aria-label="Portals" class="noprint portalbox portalborder portalright">

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Slovenia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=61" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

Source: <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://bos.zrc-sazu.si/c/SP/neva.exe?name=sp&amp;expression=republika+slovenija&amp;hs=1">"Slovenski pravopis 2001: Republika Slovenija"</a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Slovenski+pravopis+2001%3A+Republika+Slovenija&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fbos.zrc-sazu.si%2Fc%2FSP%2Fneva.exe%3Fname%3Dsp%26expression%3Drepublika%2Bslovenija%26hs%3D1&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASlovenia" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-cultures-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-cultures_17-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-cultures_17-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFČerne2004" class="citation book cs1">Černe, Andrej (2004). Orožen Adamič, Milan (ed.). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://zgs.zrc-sazu.si/Portals/8/Slo_Geo_Over/23.pdf"><i>Gateway to Western, Central, and Southeastern Europe</i></a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>. Slovenia: A Geographical Overview. Association of the Geographical Societies of Slovenia. p.&#160;127. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/961-6500-49-X" title="Special:BookSources/961-6500-49-X"><bdi>961-6500-49-X</bdi></a>. <q>For centuries, the territory of Slovenia has been crossed by traditional transportation routes connecting northern Europe with southern, eastern, and western Europe. Slovenia's location in the northwestern part of the Mediterranean's most inland bay on the Adriatic Sea where the Alps, the plateaus of the Dinaric Alps, and the western margins of the Pannonian Basin meet gives [it] a relatively quite advantageous traffic and geographical position distinguished by its transitional character and the links between these geographical regions. In a wider macroregional sense, this transitional character and these links have not changed since prehistoric times.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Gateway+to+Western%2C+Central%2C+and+Southeastern+Europe&amp;rft.series=Slovenia%3A+A+Geographical+Overview&amp;rft.pages=127&amp;rft.pub=Association+of+the+Geographical+Societies+of+Slovenia&amp;rft.date=2004&amp;rft.isbn=961-6500-49-X&amp;rft.aulast=%C4%8Cerne&amp;rft.aufirst=Andrej&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fzgs.zrc-sazu.si%2FPortals%2F8%2FSlo_Geo_Over%2F23.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASlovenia" class="Z3988"></span></span>
